### Phase 5 — Coordinate conventions and node transforms · owner: `GLTF-TRANSFORM` · **CRITICAL PATH**
*Entry: Phase 4 — **waived for the Track A tasks `GLTF-103`, `GLTF-113`, `GLTF-114`, `GLTF-115`**,
whose only prerequisites are their own dependency columns (§28.1). Exit: every `xf-*` fixture passes
numerically at L4.*

| ID | Title | St | Deps | Scope, evidence → acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GLTF-103 | **[P0] ADOPT the real `ModelBone` node hierarchy (§11.5 Option A)** | ✔ | GLTF-004, GLTF-005, GLTF-006 | **Not an open A-vs-B choice.** Option A is the presumptive final architecture (§11.5): prove it preserves XNA compatibility and adopt it. CNA already has `Model`, `ModelBone`, `ModelMesh::ParentBone` and a verified-correct `CopyAbsoluteBoneTransformsTo`. Option B (baking) may be adopted **only** on a demonstrated concrete blocker, **only** as an explicitly time-boxed interim step, and then it must carry its own removal task — it is never recorded as the final architecture. Makes `GLTF-129`/`GLTF-130` mandatory. **Dependency note:** this task deliberately does **not** depend on `GLTF-011`; the verdict is written after these fixes land, and the reverse dependency would be a cycle. **Accept:** a written decision naming the adopted architecture, the seven properties of §11.5 it preserves, and — if Option B is chosen — the concrete blocker, the time box and the removal task. **Critical path.** **Landed:** Option A adopted; no blocker found. `BuildSceneGraph` flattens the default scene parent-before-child with composed world transforms, and both loaders mirror it as `ModelBone`s. Vertex positions stay mesh-local, so instancing survives — `xf-shared-mesh`'s two placements share one mesh. `GLTF-129`/`GLTF-130` landed with it, as §11.5 requires. |
| GLTF-104 | Lock the XNA transform-order invariants | ✔ | GLTF-006 | `Model::Draw` uses `bone * world`; `AnimationPlayer` uses `local * parentWorld`. Both correct. **Accept:** unit tests pin them so a later change is caught. **Locked in `GltfConventionsTests.cpp`**, with operands chosen so a swap is unmistakable rather than merely wrong: `Model::Draw` binds a bone translated 5 through a world scaled 2, giving 10 one way and 5 the other; the scene graph places a child offset 1 under a parent scaled 2 and translated 100, giving 102 one way and 201 the other. A parent at the origin — the obvious fixture to reach for — cannot tell the two orders apart at all. |
| GLTF-105 | **Record "no axis or handedness conversion" as a testable invariant** | ✔ | GLTF-104 | glTF and XNA are both right-handed, +Y up, −Z forward; UV origin is top-left in both. **Accept:** `docs/gltf-conventions.md` states it, and a test asserts a +Z-facing glTF normal stays +Z. Any future axis flip must first fail a fixture. **Both landed.** `docs/gltf-conventions.md` is the one-place statement — the agreements table, the single conversion that *is* performed and why it is not an exception, and the two load-bearing multiplication orders. The test asserts positions, normals **and** UVs pass through unchanged, with every authored component distinct so a swapped or negated axis cannot land on the value it should have had and pass by coincidence. |
| GLTF-106 | Lock `ConvertGltfMatrix` | ✔ | GLTF-105 | Copies basis vectors, producing the XNA row-vector transpose of the glTF column-vector matrix. Correct — but unreached today. **Accept:** direct unit test over a non-trivial affine matrix. **Locked behaviourally, through `BuildSceneGraph`, rather than by exporting the helper for a test** — the same coverage without an API addition the `GLTF-025` gate would have to review. "Non-trivial" is load-bearing and the corpus could not supply it: `xf-matrix-node` is translation-only, and a translation-only matrix **cannot** tell a basis-vector copy from a naive full transpose, because transposing an identity rotation block gives the identity back. The test uses a rotation-plus-non-uniform-scale-plus-translation matrix, where the wrong transpose puts the translation in the fourth *column* instead of the fourth *row*. A second test cross-checks the `matrix` and TRS paths against each other, which catches a transpose present in exactly one of them — an error each path's own test would miss, because each is self-consistent. |
| GLTF-107 | `node.matrix` decoding | ✔ | GLTF-106 | `f13`/`xf-matrix-node`: `matrix = translate(4,5,6)` discarded. **Accept:** L4 world position `(5,5,6)`. **Landed with `GLTF-113`/`GLTF-114`** (defect D3, recorded `fixed` in the ledger): `BuildSceneGraph` reads each node's local transform through `cgltf_node_transform_local`, which applies the specification's own "matrix, or else TRS" exclusivity rule, so both authorings compose alike. `xf-matrix-node`'s world X range is the spec-derived `[4,5]` and `GltfConformanceL4` asserts it in full. |
| GLTF-108 | Quaternion component order | ✔ | GLTF-105 | glTF `(x,y,z,w)` → `Quaternion(X,Y,Z,W)`. **Accept:** locked by `xf-rot-*`. **Locked directly instead** — `xf-rot-*` is still ungenerated, and the invariant does not need a corpus asset to be pinned. 90° about +X, which maps +Y to +Z: under the classic `[w,x,y,z]` misread the same four numbers are a rotation about +Y that leaves +Y alone, so the test cannot pass by coincidence. An identity rotation, the obvious thing to author, is indistinguishable under both readings. |
| GLTF-109 | TRS composition order | ✅ | GLTF-108 | `S` then `R` then `T`. **Accept:** `GltfNodeTransformOrder.TrsComposesAsScaleThenRotateThenTranslate` picks scale (2,3,4), a quarter turn about X and translation (10,20,30) so that all six orderings differ, and asserts (0,1,0) → (10,20,33). |
| GLTF-110 | `matrix` and TRS mutual exclusivity | ✅ | GLTF-107 | §3.5. **Accept:** `GltfNodeTransformOrder.ANodeDeclaringBothMatrixAndTrsIsResolvedByTheSpecsOwnExclusivityRule` authors both in each JSON key order and asserts the `matrix` wins in both — the resolution must not depend on how the file was serialised, or the same model imports differently from two exporters. |
| GLTF-111 | Default TRS values | ✅ | GLTF-109 | `T=0`, `R=identity`, `S=1`. **Accept:** locked directly rather than waiting on `xf-identity` — `GltfNodeHierarchy.AnOmittedTrsComponentTakesItsSpecifiedDefaultRatherThanZero` asserts a bare node is the identity component by component (a zero *scale* also leaves the origin at the origin, so an origin check alone cannot see it) and that a translation-only node keeps unit scale. |
| GLTF-112 | World-transform propagation, applied exactly once | ✅ | GLTF-109 | `CopyAbsoluteBoneTransformsTo` is correct; the input arrived with `GLTF-114`. **Accept:** `GltfNodeHierarchy.AChainOfTranslationsComposesOncePerNodeAndNotTwice` authors +1/+2/+3 and asserts 6 at the deepest node — 3 is the parent chain never applied, 11 is it applied more than once. The same test asserts the *local* transforms survive composition, which is what lets `AnimationPlayer` recompose without first undoing a bake; `GltfSceneGraphBones` separately cross-checks the bone tree's own recomposition against these values. |
| GLTF-113 | **[P0] `MeshGroup` → mesh instances carrying `cgltf_node*` + world matrix** | ✔ | GLTF-103 | `struct MeshGroup { const cgltf_skin*; std::vector<const cgltf_mesh*>; }` has no node and no matrix (D1–D3). The per-instance identity introduced here is the **`sceneNodeIndex`** space of §15.1.2 and must not be ordered to suit any skin's GPU palette. **Accept:** the import data model carries per-instance node identity and transform. **Critical path.** **Landed:** `MeshGroup::meshes` → `MeshGroup::instances` (`MeshInstanceOut`: node, mesh, `sceneNodeIndex`, world transform, `skinned`). Group ordering and the fallback for a file whose scenes reference no mesh are unchanged. |
| GLTF-114 | **[P0] World transform reaches the model on BOTH loaders** | ✔ | GLTF-113 | `ReadGltfModel` and the `.cnj` `ModelTypeReader` both create identity root and identity per-mesh bones. **Accept:** `f1` yields X ∈ `[0,11]`; `f2` yields Y ∈ `[6,8]`. **Critical path.** **Landed:** One `ModelBone` per scene node on both loaders, bone index == `SceneGraphOut` index, mesh parented to its instancing node's bone. A **skinned** instance is parented to the identity root instead — glTF ignores a skinned mesh's own node transform — so this deliberately does not touch D8. |
| GLTF-115 | **[P0] The whole `xf-*` ladder passes at L4** | ✔ | GLTF-114 | All 17 fixtures in §11.4. **Accept:** every expected world position matches to 1e-6. **Critical path.** **Landed:** `GltfConformanceL4.CnaWorldPositionsMatchTheExpectedGeometry` now asserts every corpus fixture with no suppression; D1/D2/D3 are `fixed` in the ledger with the audit's measurements preserved under `priorActual`, and their inverted known-defect tests are deleted. |
| GLTF-116 | Negative scale: winding | ✅ | GLTF-115 | `xf-negative-scale` **now exists** (generator `defs/transforms.py`). **Accept, with the boundary stated:** a mirroring placement is *detected* — `MeshInstanceOut::mirroredEXT`, the sign of the composed world 3×3 determinant — and the loaders report it. It is **not applied**, and applying it at L5 would be wrong rather than merely unfinished: positions stay mesh-local (`GLTF-103` Option A), so one mesh can be instanced by a mirrored node and an unmirrored one — `xf-mirror-child` is that file — and both draws share one index buffer. Reversing it fixes one placement by breaking the other. The winding reversal is a per-draw `RasterizerState::CullMode` decision, the same application-owned boundary `GLTF-231` drew for `doubleSided`; unlike winding, tangent handedness is now corrected independently inside the EasyGL PBR vertex shader (`GLTF-176`). `GltfMirroring` locks the detection, the not-mirrored control, a 180° rotation (two axes negated, determinant +1 — what a per-component test gets wrong), and that the shared buffers are byte-identical across all three placements. |
| GLTF-117 | Mirrored transform through a hierarchy | ✅ | GLTF-116 | `xf-mirror-child` **now exists**: one mesh placed three times down a chain — unmirrored root, mirrored child, mirrored grandchild. **Accept:** manifest match at L4 (`GltfConformanceL4`), plus `GltfMirroring.MirroringFollowsTheComposedTransformAndNotTheInstancingNodesOwnScale` — the composed determinants are +, −, +, and the grandchild is the placement that catches the shortcut, because its own scale is negative while its composed transform is not. |
| GLTF-118 | Normal transformation under non-uniform scale | ✅ | GLTF-115 | `normal * transpose(inverse(A))`. **Accept:** already asserted, at L6 where it actually matters — `GltfConformanceL6.NormalMatrixIsTheInverseTransposeOfTheWorldUpper3x3` checks the renderer's cofactor derivation against XNA's `Matrix::Invert` over the whole corpus, and `NonUniformScaleSeparatesTheNormalMatrixFromTheWorldMatrix` proves `xf-scale-nonuniform` can actually fail: the two derivations send its (0.6,0.8,0) normal in measurably different directions. Recorded here rather than duplicated. |
| GLTF-119 | Tangent transformation and `w` sign under mirroring | ✅ | GLTF-118 | `tangent.w *= sign(det A)`. **Accept, split the same way as `GLTF-116`, and for the same reason:** the authored handedness survives import exactly — `GltfMirroring.AnAuthoredTangentHandednessSurvivesImportUnchanged` reads `w` back out of the packed stride-48 vertex at the offset the canonical layout table gives (never a typed constant — `GLTF-278`) — and the mirroring sign flip is per *draw*, because the vertex buffer is shared between a mirrored and an unmirrored placement exactly as the index buffer is. `TheSharedIndexAndVertexBuffersAreIdenticalForMirroredAndUnmirroredPlacements` asserts both buffers stay placement-independent, so a future per-instance flip fails loudly here instead of corrupting the other placements. `GLTF-176` now proves the draw half: EasyGL multiplies the local sign by the world, optional instance and blended-skin determinant signs in the vertex program. |
| GLTF-120 | Nodes without a mesh (transform-only) | ✅ | GLTF-114 | **Accept:** `GltfNodeHierarchy.ATransformOnlyNodePlacesItsMeshBearingChild` — a pivot at Y=7 with a mesh child at Z=2 yields an instance at (0,7,2). Empties are how every DCC tool expresses a pivot, so an importer that walked `data->meshes` or skipped mesh-less nodes would misplace most real files with no diagnostic. |
| GLTF-121 | `unitScale` interaction with node transforms | ✅ | GLTF-114 | **Was a real gap, now closed.** `GLTF-114` gave the node translations somewhere to live (the `.cnj` `"bones"` array), and the offline tool was writing them **unscaled** while `ExtractMesh` scaled every vertex — a centimetre model whose parts each shrank correctly and then stood a hundred times too far apart. The tool now emits `ScaleLocalTranslation(node.localTransform, unitScale)`; scaling each local translation scales every composed world translation by the same factor, because composition only ever adds an already-scaled parent translation to a child's. **Accept:** `GltfToCnjToolTest.UnitScaleReachesTheNodeHierarchyAndNotOnlyTheVertices` asserts vertices *and* both node translations convert together (200 cm → 2, 300 cm → 3); the existing `UnitScaleAppliesToPositionsAndBoneTranslations` still passes. |
| GLTF-122 | Multiple root nodes | ✅ | GLTF-114 | **Accept:** `GltfNodeHierarchy.EveryRootOfAMultiRootSceneIsImportedAndPlaced` — three roots at x = −5/0/5, all parented to the synthetic root, all three placements distinct, and one mesh instanced three times. The failure it is written against is the shortcut of treating `scene.nodes[0]` as *the* root, which imports a third of the file and discards the rest. |
| GLTF-123 | Node visited from two scenes | ✔ | GLTF-114 | `CollectSceneReachableNodes` de-duplicates. **Accept:** locked. **Locked — and writing it corrected the row's premise.** §3.5.3 makes the node hierarchy a disjoint union of strict *trees*, so a node cannot have two **parents** at all; cgltf rejects that document outright, and it is not the shape this row is about. What a file legitimately can do is name the same node twice among one scene's roots, and list it as a root of several scenes. Both are de-duplicated, asserted at the graph *and* the extraction layer — a graph that de-duplicates but an extractor that does not would still import every mesh twice, drawing geometry on top of itself. |
| GLTF-124 | Cycle / malformed hierarchy guard | ✅ | GLTF-114 | **Accept:** a cyclic `children` graph errors rather than hanging — proved for both shapes a cycle can take. A cycle *reachable from a scene root* gives that root a parent, and cgltf refuses it during pointer fixup, so it never reaches CNA (`ACycleReachableFromASceneRootIsRefusedBeforeAnyTraversalRunsAtAll`). A cycle among nodes **no scene lists** is structurally consistent and parses; `ValidateGltfEXT` rejects it, and `BuildSceneGraph` never walks it because it walks scenes, not `data->nodes` (`ACycleOutsideEverySceneParsesButIsRejectedAndNeverWalked`). Reaching the end of either test is itself the termination proof. |
| GLTF-125 | Deep-hierarchy depth limit | ✅ | GLTF-124 | The traversal is iterative (stack-based). **Accept:** `GltfNodeHierarchy.ATenThousandDeepChainDoesNotOverflowTheStack` builds a 10 000-level chain and asserts both the node count and the composed translation, so a refactor back to recursion — shorter, and passing every other test in the directory — fails here. |
| GLTF-126 | Bone naming and index stability | ✅ | GLTF-113 | Nodes without a `name` get `Node<index>` from the traversal order. **Accept:** `GltfNodeHierarchy.UnnamedNodesGetStableDistinctGeneratedNames` builds the same document twice and asserts name-for-name equality, mutual distinctness (bone lookup by name is ambiguous otherwise, and `.cnj` serializes bones by name) and that an authored name passes through untouched. |
| GLTF-127 | `Model::Root` is the scene root | ✅ | GLTF-114 | **Accept:** already asserted by `GltfSceneGraphBonesTests`' contract sweep, over every corpus fixture rather than one: `Model::Root` is bone 0, bone 0 is the synthetic identity root (no parent, name `Root`, identity transform), and `CopyAbsoluteBoneTransformsTo`'s recomposition from the bone tree is cross-checked against `BuildSceneGraph`'s own composed world transforms — two independent computations that must not drift. Recorded here rather than duplicated. |
| GLTF-128 | World-space model bounds exposed to callers | ✔ | GLTF-114 | The viewer currently recomputes bounds from vertex sidecars. **Accept:** a CNAEXT bounds accessor; `GLTF-427` consumes it. **Landed and now consumed:** `Model::getBoundingSphereEXTProperty()` transforms every existing `ModelMesh::BoundingSphere` by its current absolute parent-bone matrix — the same placement `Model::Draw` uses — and merges them, returning `std::nullopt` only for a model with no meshes. For a mesh listed in `Model::SkinsEXT`, it additionally unions the local sphere under every matrix in the current `SkinnedEffect`/`SkinnedPbrEffect` palette before mesh placement. That is conservative for linear blend skinning and, crucially, follows glTF's mesh-node cancellation: the real `skin-mesh-node-transform` viewer retake first drew correctly at z=−50 while the placement-only camera looked at z=0. The radius uses a conservative maximum-stretch bound for composed shear, which a rotated child below a non-uniformly scaled parent can produce and XNA's ordinary longest-basis shortcut can underbound. The space is stated precisely: glTF's composed/posed model-root space, before the application `world` matrix that only the caller knows. Both direct glTF and offline `.cnj` loading initialise the local mesh sphere from **all** primitives grouped into that `ModelMesh`, using the authored default morph pose when non-zero weights exist; no `.cnj` schema change is needed because the vertex sidecars already carry the positions. `ModelTest.BoundingSphereEXTIncludesTheCurrentSkinPaletteBeforeMeshPlacement` locks a live palette update, `GltfSkinSpaces.MeshNodeTransformIsCancelledExactlyOnce` locks the real z=−50 fixture bound, the earlier rigid/shear/model-shape tests retain their coverage, and the viewer capture is the L7 witness. A later morph still keeps XNA's existing contract: the caller updates the read-write mesh sphere if geometry escapes it. |
| GLTF-129 | `.cnj` format: `"bones"` + per-mesh `"parentBone"` | ✔ | GLTF-103, GLTF-025 | Additive, `cnjVersion` bumped. **Accept:** old `.cnj` files still load; new ones round-trip at L4. **Landed:** `cnjVersion` 2 adds `"bones"` (name/parent/local transform) and per-mesh `"parentBone"`. The version ceiling is **per type** (`ValidateCnjEnvelope(..., maxVersion)`) — only `Model` accepts 2 — so "an unknown future version is rejected" stays true for every other `.cnj` type. A version-1 Model still loads through the same reader and keeps its per-mesh child-bone behaviour. |
| GLTF-130 | `.cnj` round-trip equals the runtime path at L4 | ✔ | GLTF-129 | The two loaders must not diverge. **Accept:** every corpus fixture yields identical L4 output via both paths. **Landed:** Both loaders build the same tree from the same `SceneGraphOut`; the offline path round-trips it through `"bones"`/`"parentBone"`. Asserted by the shared L4 suite, which runs over the corpus rather than per loader. |
| GLTF-131 | Transform fixtures in `.glb` form too | ✅ | GLTF-115, GLTF-026 | **Accept:** `GltfConformanceL4.TheGlbAndGltfTwinsPlaceTheSameGeometry` imports both containers of every corpus fixture through CNA's own path and compares node, mesh, composed world matrix and placed vertices **byte for byte**. The existing GLB test compared structural *counts*; this asks the question the row is about, over a path where the JSON is a chunk and every buffer resolves to the BIN chunk rather than a URI. Exact equality is the right bar — the two files state the same numbers, so any difference is a container bug, not accumulated rounding. |
| GLTF-132 | Document the transform pipeline in `docs/` | ✅ | GLTF-115 | **Accept:** `docs/gltf-conventions.md` now carries the pipeline stage-by-stage (which stage owns each step, from `node.matrix` to `uNormalMatrix`), the equations in both conventions (`S·R·T`, `local * parentWorld`, `bone * callerWorld`, `transpose(inverse(World₃ₓ₃))`), the TRS defaults **next to the wrong default each one is mistaken for**, the mirroring rule with the reason it is detected rather than applied, and what `unitScale` does and does not touch. |

### Phase 6 — Scenes, instancing, model shape · owner: `GLTF-TRANSFORM`
*Entry: Phase 5. Exit: a shared mesh instanced by N nodes produces N correctly-placed instances.*

| ID | Title | St | Deps | Scope, evidence → acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GLTF-133 | Default-scene selection | ✔ | GLTF-114 | `f14` proved `scene: 1` is honoured and the decoy excluded. **Accept:** locked as `scene-default-selection`. **Locked twice over:** the corpus fixture, plus a scratch document whose decoy carries its own mesh rather than being an empty node — so defaulting to scene 0, the obvious shortcut, loads entirely the wrong content instead of merely the wrong node count. |
| GLTF-134 | No `scenes` array at all | ✅ | GLTF-133 | Falls back to "every mesh in the file". **Accept:** `GltfModelShape.AFileWithNoScenesFallsBackToEveryMeshInIt` — §3.9 makes `scenes` optional, and an exporter asked for geometry rather than a scene writes exactly this. The graph is the synthetic root alone, every mesh is imported in file order, and each placement is explicitly attributed to no node and to the root index, because there is no node to place it by. Refusing the file, or importing nothing from it, are both worse outcomes than the documented fallback. |
| GLTF-135 | Nodes outside any scene are excluded | ✔ | GLTF-133 | `OnlyImportsNodesReachableFromTheDefaultScene` exists. **Accept:** extended to the corpus. **The existing test is a `GltfToCnjToolTest`, which this configuration SKIPS** — so the rule was effectively unasserted here. Now locked directly at graph and extraction level: an exporter routinely leaves helpers, proxies and hidden geometry in the `nodes` pool without putting them in a scene, and importing those drags back in exactly what the author took out. |
| GLTF-136 | Shared mesh instanced by multiple nodes | ✔ | GLTF-114 | `f1`: today two identity-transform duplicates. **Accept:** `xf-shared-mesh` yields two instances at the right places, ideally sharing one `VertexBuffer`. **Landed with `GLTF-113`/`GLTF-114`** (defect D1, `fixed`): the two instances are placed 10 units apart and the union spans the spec-derived X range `[0,11]`, asserted by `GltfConformanceL4`. **The "ideally" half is deliberately not claimed:** the loader still extracts and uploads a separate `VertexBuffer` per instance, so a mesh instanced *n* times occupies *n* copies. That is a memory and load-time cost, not a correctness one — the bytes are identical, which `GLTF-007`'s L5 goldens would catch if they ever were not — and de-duplicating it means keying uploads by `(mesh, primitive)` inside the import loop. Recorded here rather than silently satisfied by the correctness half. |
| GLTF-137 | Runtime path imports **all** mesh groups, not `groups.front()` | ✔ | GLTF-114 | `ReadGltfModel` documents the single-group limit; a mixed skinned + static file silently loses a group. **Accept:** one `Model` carries every group, or the limitation is removed by the node-hierarchy work. **Landed:** the loader builds the list of importable instances once — every group's, each paired with the skeleton that poses it — instead of taking `groups.front()`, so a file with a character and a prop keeps both. `skin-plus-static-mesh` is the smallest file that exhibited the drop and is new to §15.4 (see the count note in §24.2); the skinned node is authored first, so the **static** prop is what used to vanish, and the L6 capture asserts both parts are drawable, keep their own skinned/unskinned nature, and that the prop lands at its own node's placement as stated at L4. **One limit survives and is now reported instead of hidden:** `Model::Tag` holds a single `SkinningData`, so a *second* skin's meshes are still not imported — posing them with another rig's palette would be a fresh silent corruption in place of the old silent drop, the same reasoning `GLTF-295` records for rigid clips. The unskinned group is never affected. |
| GLTF-138 | Grouping by skin becomes a detail, not the model shape | ✅ | GLTF-137 | **Decision recorded: the two are different questions, and only one of them was ever the model shape.** `Model`'s shape is now settled by `GLTF-139` — one `ModelMesh` per placement, one `ModelMeshPart` per primitive — and it no longer references a skin group at all. What remains skin-grouped is the offline tool's *file packaging*: one `.cnj` per skin, because `Model::Tag` holds one object and a skinned model's `Tag` is its `SkinningData` (`docs/gltf-api-change-review.md` §1.5, the same constraint `GLTF-295` reports against). Merging the groups into one `.cnj` would therefore need a second `Tag`, which XNA does not have. Kept as is, and now recorded as packaging rather than shape. |
| GLTF-139 | `ModelMesh` ↔ glTF mesh ↔ primitive mapping | ✅ | GLTF-114 | **Chosen: XNA's shape, and both loaders now build it** — one `ModelMesh` per *placement*, one `ModelMeshPart` per primitive. A `ModelMesh` owns the `ParentBone` and `BoundingSphere` its parts share, so the old per-primitive mesh gave a two-material object two of each and made it look like two objects. The `.cnj` "meshes" array is per primitive, so a multi-primitive entry now carries an additive `"partOfMesh"` grouping key; a file without it (cnjVersion 1, every hand-written `.model.json`, every single-primitive mesh) still gets one `ModelMesh` per entry, unchanged. **Accept:** `GltfModelShape.AMultiPrimitiveMeshBecomesOneModelMeshWithOnePartPerPrimitive` on the runtime path and `GltfToCnjToolTest.AMultiPrimitiveMeshRoundTripsAsOneModelMeshWithTwoParts` on the offline one, so `GLTF-130`'s two-loader agreement covers the shape too. **Found while landing it:** a `ModelMeshPart` registers its effect on the mesh that *owns* it, and a part built before its mesh exists has no owner — so building the mesh after its parts silently left `ModelMesh::Effects` empty, and `Model::Draw` binds World/View/Projection through exactly that collection. Both loaders now attach effects after construction, and the L6 capture is what caught it. |
| GLTF-140 | Deterministic mesh/part ordering | ✅ | GLTF-139 | **Accept:** `GltfModelShape.MeshAndPartOrderIsTheFilesOwnAndIsStableAcrossLoads` loads the same file through two `ContentManager`s and compares mesh identity in order. The ordering is the glTF node array's (what `CollectMeshGroups` documents) then each mesh's own primitive order — never a hash of a pointer, which is stable within a run and different in the next one, the failure mode that makes a golden comparison flap with no code change at all. |
| GLTF-141 | Mesh/node/part naming | ✅ | GLTF-126 | **Accept:** a `ModelMesh` takes the glTF mesh's name, falling back to its node's before it falls back to an index (a node is far more often named than a mesh), and a mesh placed by several nodes carries the placing node's name too — otherwise two placements of one mesh are identically named and `Model.Meshes["Tri"]` is a coin flip. The offline path now writes a per-primitive `"name"` as well, instead of leaving every imported mesh called "mesh". Locked by `GltfModelShape.AMeshIsNamedAfterTheGltfMeshItInstances` and `TwoPlacementsOfOneMeshAreNamedApartByTheirNodes`. |
| GLTF-142 | Empty mesh / empty primitive handling | ✅ | GLTF-139 | **Accept:** deterministic, reported, no crash — `GltfModelShape.AFileWhoseOnlyMeshHasNoPrimitivesIsRefusedByName` gets a named `ContentLoadException` rather than an empty `Model`, which would draw nothing and say nothing and be indistinguishable from a camera pointing the wrong way. The degenerate *index count* case is `GLTF-079`'s, and it is reported rather than refused, because there the file does describe drawable geometry. |
| GLTF-143 | A file with meshes but no scene node referencing them | ✔ | GLTF-134 | Existing fallback. **Accept:** locked. **Locked with the distinction that makes it consistent with `GLTF-135` rather than contradicting it:** what is excluded is a node **no scene reaches**, not a mesh no node uses. A separate test proves the fallback does *not* fire for a file that has scenes — otherwise an excluded orphan's mesh would come straight back in through the other door, and both rows would still look green. |
| GLTF-144 | Camera and light nodes must not become mesh instances | ✅ | GLTF-114 | **Accept:** asserted over the three camera fixtures the corpus actually has (`camera-perspective`, `camera-orthographic`, `camera-animated-node`) in `GltfModelShape.ACameraOrLightNodeNeverBecomesAMeshInstance`: each fixture is first checked to still *contain* a camera or light node, then every placement is required to have a mesh and to come from a node that instances one. The walk keys on `node.mesh`, but it is a scene walk — one that counted nodes instead would create a placement for the camera, and that placement would either dereference null or draw whatever mesh index 0 happens to be. A punctual-light fixture is not yet in the corpus; the node-level rule is the same one and is covered here. |
| GLTF-145 | Node-graph import report | ✅ | GLTF-034 | Node count, instance count, depth, shared-mesh count — plus distinct meshes, camera nodes, light nodes and `EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing` nodes. **Accept:** `NodeGraphReportEXT`/`BuildNodeGraphReportEXT` populate every field and `ContentManager` logs them. The computation-oriented type remains internal and now feeds `GltfImportReportEXT`, so public callers receive the same counts without depending on cgltf-oriented state. Locked by `GltfNodeHierarchy.TheNodeGraphReportCountsWhatTheFileActuallyContains`, authored so no two counts are equal — a report wired to its own neighbours' values would otherwise pass — and by `TheReportOfAnEmptySceneIsZeroRatherThanOne`, because the synthetic root makes the obvious `nodes.size()` say one. |
| GLTF-146 | `EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing` interaction placeholder | ✅ | GLTF-136 | CNA already has `DrawInstancedPrimitives`, so this is implementable and simply is not implemented (`GLTF-352`). **Accept:** no longer silently ignored — nodes carrying it are counted into the node-graph report and the loader warns with that count, because a forest authored as one mesh with a hundred instance transforms renders as *one tree*, and one tree reads as missing content rather than as an unsupported extension. `IsGltfExtensionSupportedEXT` still returns false for it, so a file that **requires** it is refused up front. Locked by `GltfNodeHierarchy.GpuInstancingNodesAreCountedSoTheyCanBeReported`. |
| GLTF-147 | Very large node counts | ✅ | GLTF-125 | **Accept:** `GltfNodeHierarchy.TenThousandNodesImportWithinTheStatedBudget` — 10 000 scene roots through `BuildSceneGraph` and `CollectMeshGroups`, **budget 2000 ms**, measured at ~33 ms. The budget is deliberately generous because the number is there to catch an accidental quadratic (a per-node linear scan of the node array would put this in minutes), not to police milliseconds. `GLTF-125` covered depth; this is breadth, the other axis of the same question. |
| GLTF-148 | Scene-level L4 regression for the whole corpus | ✅ | GLTF-115 | **Accept:** `GltfConformanceL4.CnaWorldPositionsMatchTheExpectedGeometry` already asserts every non-rejection fixture's full world-space vertex set against the spec-derived manifest, with no suppression, and `TheGlbAndGltfTwinsPlaceTheSameGeometry` (`GLTF-131`) repeats it out of the other container. Recorded here rather than duplicated. |

### Phase 7 — GPU packing and the stride ABI · owner: `CNA-GPU-PACKING`
*Entry: Phase 6. Exit: every generated buffer is byte-exact against a golden, on every renderer.*

| ID | Title | St | Deps | Scope, evidence → acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GLTF-149 | Golden vertex-buffer bytes for all seven strides | ✅ | GLTF-007 | **Accept:** `memcmp` clean, and a one-byte change reported at the right *field* — `BufferDifference` maps the first differing byte through the canonical stride table and names the attribute, which `GltfBufferOracle` tests directly. **Six of the seven strides now have goldens.** 24, 48 and 68 already did; this task added the three that were unreachable because metallic-roughness is glTF's default in two separate ways, so nothing in the corpus could select a non-PBR layout: `mat-unlit` (32), `skin-unlit` (52) and `skin-vertex-color` (56). The generator's `select_stride` learned the non-PBR material models to do it, and still **raises rather than guesses** on a shape it does not model. **Stride 20 landed too**, once `GLTF-190` gave the corpus a PNG encoder: `tex-dual-texture-stride` is a non-PBR material carrying both a base-colour and an occlusion texture, which is the only combination a texture decides a stride for. **All seven strides the importer can emit now have byte-exact goldens.** The fixture also exercises the corpus's new *dropped-attribute* declaration: stride 20 has no normal or tangent slot, so the manifest states the authored streams at full value, names them as dropped with the reason, and the comparison asserts they really came back empty — "documented limitation" must not become cover for "present and wrong". |
| GLTF-150 | Position at offset 0 for every stride | ✔ | GLTF-149 | **Accept:** static assertion + L5 test. **Locked with `GLTF-155`** — `EveryStrideTheGltfImporterCanEmitIsInTheCanonicalTable` walks all eight strides the importer can produce and asserts Position at offset 0 on each, per stride rather than assumed once; `GLTF-083` adds the L5 half, that the bytes there really are the authored positions. |
| GLTF-151 | Element offsets match §2.3 exactly | ✅ | GLTF-149 | **Accept:** one assertion per element of each of the eight strides — usage, offset, format and usage index — in `GltfStrideAndBuffer.EveryCanonicalStrideHasExactlyTheElementsSection23States`, with the values **written out** rather than recomputed: a test that derived them the way the table does would agree with any value at all. Two properties no per-element assertion states get their own tests: every element fits inside its own record (one that overruns reads into the next vertex, so the mesh looks progressively wronger along the buffer), and an unlisted stride comes back `known == false` rather than with a plausible guess. |
| GLTF-152 | `ModelMeshPart` counts and offsets | ✔ | GLTF-078 | `PrimitiveCount = numIndices/3` unconditionally in all three loaders. **Accept:** the §12.3 table, asserted per topology; `VertexOffset`/`StartIndex` remain 0 and are tested. **Landed:** the count half by `GLTF-078`'s topology-aware helper, asserted per topology against each fixture's own `importPolicy.primitiveCount`. The offsets had no test at all and now do, over every part of every corpus fixture — together with the fact that makes a zero offset *correct* rather than merely true: the loader gives each part its own buffers, so `NumVertices` spans the whole vertex buffer. A future change that packs several parts into one buffer has to set the offsets rather than inherit a silent 0. |
| GLTF-153 | `IndexBuffer` element size matches `use32BitIndices` | ✅ | GLTF-149 | **Accept:** `GltfStrideAndBuffer.TheIndexBufferElementSizeFollowsTheVertexCountOnBothSides` builds a 65 535- and a 65 536-vertex mesh, loads each through the runtime path and reads `IndexElementSize` off the created `IndexBuffer`. `GLTF-069` locked the importer's choice; this is the half that reaches the GPU, where the two wrong answers both render: 16-bit over 32-bit data draws every second index as garbage, 32-bit over 16-bit reads two indices as one. |
| GLTF-154 | Colour byte quantisation rule | ✔ | GLTF-089 | `round(clamp(f,0,1)*255)`. **Accept:** endpoints asserted. **Locked on a FLOAT `COLOR_0`, which is the only shape that can test it** — a normalized-integer colour is already a byte and round-trips under any rule, which is why the existing `normalized-u8-color` coverage proved nothing about the rounding. Truncation and rounding agree at 0 and 1 and differ everywhere between, so mid values carry the test: 0.5 → 128 (not 127), 0.002 → 1 (not 0), 0.998 → 254. The **clamp** gets its own test: unclamped, 1.5 narrows to 126, so a bright colour would arrive as mid grey and read as a material bug rather than an out-of-range input. |
| GLTF-155 | **One canonical stride → `VertexDeclaration` table** | ✔ | GLTF-151 | Today the table lives in `ExtractMesh` and is re-implemented as a `switch (stride)` in every renderer, with no shared source of truth. **Accept:** a single declaration in `modules/graphics`, consumed by the importer and available to renderers. **The table is `CNA/Internal/Graphics/VertexDeclarationFidelity.hpp`'s `InferredLayoutForStride` — header-only, in `modules/graphics`, already consumed by renderers.** What this task added is the importer side: its unlisted-stride refusal is now a *query against the table* rather than a literal list, and a test asserts the two sets are one — every stride the importer can emit is in the table, and an unlisted stride comes back `known == false` rather than with a guessed layout. That coupling is what `GLTF-278` proved is needed: a restated ABI goes stale silently. The per-stride **upload** branches necessarily stay explicit — each selects a distinct C++ vertex struct, which a data table cannot do — and complete per-renderer declaration *translation* remains `plan_postaudit.md`'s, as that header's own documentation states. |
| GLTF-156 | Static assertions tying the table to the vertex structs | ✅ | GLTF-155 | **Accept:** `modules/graphics/src/Internal/VertexDeclarationFidelityStaticChecks.cpp` — every element of every stride paired with `offsetof` on the struct that produces those bytes, so a field reordering fails the **build**, with the field named. **Corrected while writing it:** the structs to tie the table to are the packed GPU streams in `BuiltInVertexStreams.hpp`, not the public `VertexPosition*` types. Those are not the buffer layout at all — `Color` alone is 24 bytes of packed-vector machinery, so `sizeof(VertexPositionColor)` is 40, not 16 — and every built-in `VertexDeclaration` already takes its own offsets from the streams. Stride 56 has no stream struct (the importer writes those bytes itself); it is listed with that reason and its trailing Color pinned, so "not asserted" is a visible decision. |
| GLTF-157 | Replace the silent position-only `ApplyLayout` fallback | ✅ | GLTF-155 | An unknown stride left normals/UVs/weights **unbound**, reading stale VAO state. **Closed on both boundaries.** `BuildVertexBufferFromRawBytes` already rejects an unlisted importer stride by name instead of returning an empty buffer. The formerly unavailable `../easy-gl` and `../meta-gl` sibling checkouts are now present, so the renderer half is compiled and exercised too: `EasyGLRenderer::ApplyLayout` unbinds its VAO and throws `NotSupportedException`, naming the stride and missing `VertexDeclaration`; custom layouts still use the generic declaration path above it. `EasyGL_UnknownStride_Rejected` creates a real OPENGLES3 context under Xvfb, uploads one raw stride-28 record and asserts the exact refusal. It passes on Mesa OpenGL ES 3.2; no debug-only `CNA_RENDER_LOG` is involved. |
| GLTF-158 | Renderer stride-conformance test | ✅ | GLTF-157 | **Accept:** every enabled renderer accepts all seven strides and binds every element; run in CI for `STUB`, `HEADLESS`, `OPENGLES3`, `VULKAN`. **Closed by two complementary tests.** `GltfStrideAndBuffer.EveryImportedGltfStrideCarriesItsCanonicalVertexDeclaration` loads one real corpus witness for each of strides 20/24/32/48/52/56/68 and compares the complete public declaration against the canonical table. `RendererStrideConformance.EveryGltfStrideReachesTheNativeDrawBoundary` then performs `Model::Draw` + `Present` for every witness: HEADLESS validates all seven, Mesa OpenGL ES 3.2 binds the declaration-driven EasyGL layout, and Vulkan/lavapipe creates, records and submits every matching native pipeline. STUB runs the declaration/upload half and explicitly skips the impossible native-draw half because `ThreeD=false`. `.github/workflows/gltf-renderer-stride-ci.yml` runs exactly this four-renderer matrix per relevant commit. |
| GLTF-159 | Pass a real `VertexDeclaration` to the renderer boundary | ✅ | GLTF-155 | `ApplyLayout` already has a generic declaration path; the glTF loader never populated one. **Decision: adopted.** `BuildVertexBufferFromRawBytes` now queries `InferredLayoutForStride`, constructs the ordered `VertexElement` list and creates the `VertexBuffer` with that declaration before any typed or raw upload. `VertexBuffer::UploadValidatedData` propagates it on every branch. EasyGL therefore takes its generic declaration path; Vulkan retains it for `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT`; magic strides survive only as renderer-internal pipeline details. The seven-fixture test would have failed on the former capacity-only constructor's empty declaration. |
| GLTF-160 | Audit every renderer's stride table against §2.3 | ✅ | GLTF-158 | **Accept:** a per-renderer conformance matrix in `docs/`. **Closed by `docs/gltf-renderer-stride-conformance.md`.** It writes out all seven semantic/offset/format sequences, distinguishes a public declaration from an actual native binding, and records STUB/HEADLESS as honest non-rasterizing N/A cases. EasyGL's generic formatter/binder and stock-program declaration guard were checked element by element; Vulkan's 20/24/32/48/52/56/68 tables were checked for locations, offsets and `UNORM` Color versus integer BlendIndices, then exercised by real submit/present. |
| GLTF-161 | `BuildVertexBufferFromRawBytes` covers every stride | ✅ | GLTF-155 | Correct for the seven known strides. **Accept:** locked, both halves. An unknown stride throws (it used to return the freshly constructed, empty buffer as though it had been filled — `GLTF-157`), and the failure reaches a caller as a named `ContentLoadException` rather than an empty part, asserted by `GltfStrideAndBuffer.AnUnbuildableVertexBufferSurfacesAsAContentLoadExceptionRatherThanACrash`. Six of the seven strides additionally have byte-exact goldens through the corpus (`GLTF-149`). |
| GLTF-162 | `VertexBuffer::SetDataRaw` contract | ✅ | GLTF-161 | Used by morph re-upload. **Accept:** tested, and the contract written down where a caller reads it. `count * stride` bytes land where the stride says (the second vertex at offset 32 is what proves the stride was honoured rather than the element size guessed), and the refusals — count above capacity, null data, negative count, zero stride — either throw or write nothing. **Stated explicitly because a test cannot cover it:** the *source extent* is the caller's promise. A buffer built without a `VertexDeclaration` has a capacity in vertices and none in bytes, so `SetDataRaw(p, 2, 48)` is a legal request to read 96 bytes and CNA cannot know whether `p` has them. That is why the morph path derives both numbers from one `MeshOut`. |
| GLTF-163 | 32-bit index support on every renderer | ✔ | GLTF-153 | Some legacy renderers may not support `ThirtyTwoBits`. **Accept:** capability matrix; a clear error rather than silent truncation. **Closed across all 42 renderer directories.** `docs/graphics-renderer-feature-matrix.md` now records every renderer as exact native/CPU support, observable non-rasterising support, conditional GLES1 support, or explicit no-3D refusal. The unsafe shared `CreateIndexBuffer32 → CreateIndexBuffer16` delegation is gone: the default throws before upload/draw, while PortableGL, SDL_GPU and Stub — the three real uint32 consumers that had relied on that delegation — now own width-specific factories. PortableGL and Stub additionally reject renderer-level cross-width uploads. GLES1 keeps its `GL_OES_element_index_uint` gate, but the absent-extension branch now reaches the clear shared error rather than a disguised 16-bit buffer. `GltfRendererIndexWidthPolicy` enumerates the 42 directories, locks the 31 providers + 2 local rejecters + 9 inherited rejecters, checks the GLES1 gate and prevents an unaudited future renderer; the shared fallback's exact runtime diagnostic is independently exercised through a qualified base call. Existing `GltfIndexForm` and `GltfStrideAndBuffer` witnesses retain `65536` and values above 16 bits through import and the real `IndexBuffer`. Compile-verified on SOFTWARE plus the three changed backend libraries (STUB, SDL_GPU, PortableGL). |
| GLTF-164 | Large-mesh boundary fixture (65 535 / 65 536 vertices) | ✅ | GLTF-069 | Generated in the test, not committed — 800 KB of positions apiece. **Accept:** both sides decode *and* reach a drawable part: `NumVertices` equals the authored count and the one triangle, drawn from the last three vertices so it addresses the very top of the index range, survives as `PrimitiveCount == 1`. Asserted alongside `GLTF-153` in the same test, because the boundary and the element size are the same boundary. |
| GLTF-165 | Buffer creation failure handling | ✅ | GLTF-161 | **Accept:** `GltfStrideAndBuffer.AnUnbuildableVertexBufferSurfacesAsAContentLoadExceptionRatherThanACrash`. A genuine host allocation failure cannot be provoked deterministically in a unit test, so what is asserted is the property that depends on it: an unbuildable buffer leaves `Load` as a **named exception** carrying the file, and never as a silently empty part. That is the same guarantee, at the boundary a caller meets — and the specific hole it closes is the one `GLTF-157` found, where an unlisted stride fell out of the branch chain and an empty `VertexBuffer` was returned as though it had been filled. |
| GLTF-166 | Zero-vertex / zero-index primitive | ✅ | GLTF-079 | **Accept:** no zero-size buffer creation, deterministic behaviour, both halves tested. A zero-count POSITION accessor either fails by name or produces no part at all — never a part reporting zero vertices. A two-index `TRIANGLES` run (no whole triangle in it) is trimmed to nothing by `GLTF-079` and the drop is reported, rather than reaching a zero-length `IndexBuffer` allocation, which is undefined on several backends. |
| GLTF-167 | L5 golden regeneration procedure | ✅ | GLTF-020 | **Accept:** documented in `tools/gltf_fixtures/README.md`. Goldens regenerate through the ordinary `--out` command (there is no separate golden step) and `--check` proves the committed tree is byte-identical to what regeneration produces. Two properties make the diff reviewable: the generator **never reads CNA** — it packs from the manifest's own L3 streams, so a golden can disagree with the importer, which is the whole point of having one — and the reviewable artefact is the `.expected.json` block, which states stride, field offsets and vertex count in text, with the `.bin` files following from it. The section also states which stride has no golden and why. |
| GLTF-168 | Cross-renderer L5 equality | ✅ | GLTF-158 | L5 is renderer-independent by construction. **Accept:** byte-identical across `STUB`/`HEADLESS`/`OPENGLES3`/`VULKAN`; any divergence is a bug. **All four are byte-identical.** `scripts/gltf-renderer-parity.sh` reports the same 42 L1–L5 oracle results for Vulkan versus each of STUB, HEADLESS and OPENGLES3; earlier pairings already covered the other edges. Every run compares with the same committed goldens, so this is stronger than two renderers merely agreeing with each other. The new draw-boundary test deliberately lives outside the L5 suite: STUB's legitimate `ThreeD=false` skip cannot masquerade as a byte divergence. |

### Phase 8 — Normals and tangents · owner: `GLTF-MESH`
*Entry: Phase 7. Exit: shading basis is correct under every transform in the corpus.*

| ID | Title | St | Deps | Scope, evidence → acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GLTF-169 | Authored `TANGENT` is used verbatim when present | ✔ | GLTF-086 | **Accept:** `tangent-authored` matches at L5, `w` included. **Locked at the byte level, with the authored tangent deliberately chosen NOT to match what generation would produce for the same UVs** — otherwise "carried" and "recomputed" are the same answer and the test proves nothing. The `w` is the half that gets lost: it is not a direction but a **sign**, the bitangent's orientation decided by the UV winding the author baked in. Recomputing it, or defaulting it to +1, mirrors every normal-mapped detail on any surface whose UVs are flipped — which reads as an art bug, not an importer one. |
| GLTF-170 | Tangent generation when absent | ✔ | GLTF-169 | `ComputeTangentsEXT`: angle-weighted, Gram-Schmidt, documented non-MikkTSpace divergence. **Accept:** `tangent-absent-generated` matches the manifest; the divergence stays documented. **Locked on direction *and* length, now with the named corpus fixture rather than only an inline probe.** Its geometry and UV axes make the answer exactly `(+X,+1)` at every vertex, so the L5 golden needs no second implementation of the tangent algorithm. The manifest keeps that answer under `importPolicy.generatedTangents`, distinct from the authored `tangents` field, and a direct production-path test proves CNA generated it from an asset that has `TEXCOORD_0` but no `TANGENT` accessor. Unit length and perpendicularity are asserted separately because a Gram-Schmidt step that forgets to renormalise leaves a shorter vector, which does not look obviously wrong: it silently changes normal-map lighting. The MikkTSpace divergence remains `GLTF-179`'s to quantify. |
| GLTF-171 | Tangent generation is gated on `usePbr` | ✔ | GLTF-170, GLTF-215 | After the PBR-selection fix, far more meshes need tangents. **Accept:** generation is driven by the *effect's* need, not by map presence. **Landed with `GLTF-215`:** the gate is still spelled `usePbr`, and that is now exactly the acceptance rather than a violation of it — `usePbr` **is** the effect selection since `GLTF-215` replaced the map-presence rule with the material model the file declares, and strides 48/68 are the only layouts with a Tangent slot at all, so generating for anything else would write into another field. The consequence is the one the task predicted: `mat-factor-only-gold` carries no map of any kind and still gets a tangent stream, which its byte-exact stride-48 L5 golden proves. |
| GLTF-172 | Degenerate-UV triangles contribute nothing | ✔ | GLTF-170 | `fabs(denom) < 1e-12` skip. **Accept:** no NaN/Inf in any generated tangent, asserted over the corpus. **Locked with `std::isfinite`, and the choice of assertion is the point:** NaN compares false against everything, so `EXPECT_NEAR(anything, nan)` fails with a message about the wrong thing and `EXPECT_NE` **passes**. A value comparison cannot catch this defect. The fixture collapses all three UVs to one point — a real authoring outcome for untextured or collapsed geometry — so the solve divides by a zero UV area; unguarded that is 0/0, and the NaN propagates through the whole lighting term and renders as black or as nothing. The handedness is additionally asserted to still be a sign, because a basis that could not be solved must still leave `w` at ±1. |
| GLTF-173 | Missing `NORMAL`: decide generate vs default | ✔ | GLTF-084 | Today a fabricated `(0,0,1)` is written. glTF says a primitive without `NORMAL` uses flat shading. **Accept:** decision recorded; `normal-absent` matches it. **Decided: generate**, because §3.7.2.1 makes it a MUST and the old default was not a default at all — it asserted every surface faces +Z, so a model lit from any other direction was uniformly and silently wrong. CNA computes the **area-weighted vertex normal**, which for a mesh whose author already split its edges *is* the flat normal, exactly. Where a vertex is shared between faces of different orientation, true flat shading needs it duplicated once per face — that changes the vertex count and every per-vertex stream including morph deltas, which this single-vertex-array extraction cannot express — so those vertices get the average and are **counted** in `smoothedNormalVertexCountEXT`, reported by both loaders. New fixture `normal-absent` is deliberately **tilted out of the XY plane**: every other normal-less asset in the corpus is planar and CCW, so its own face normal is exactly `(0,0,1)` and none of them could ever have told the two behaviours apart — which is also why the corpus regenerated byte-identically. |
| GLTF-174 | Missing `NORMAL` + tangent generation | ✅ | GLTF-173 | **Accept:** consistent, and now locked. `ComputeTangentsEXT` receives the normals `GLTF-173` generated rather than its own `(0,0,1)` fallback, so the packed normal and the tangent basis describe the same surface. `GltfUvChannel.TangentGenerationUsesTheGeneratedNormalRatherThanAFabricatedPlusZ` authors a triangle facing **−X** — the exact opposite of the fallback, so the two orders cannot be confused — and asserts the packed normal is the triangle's own, the tangent is perpendicular to it, the tangent is not degenerate (which would satisfy perpendicularity trivially) and `w` is a sign rather than a scale. |
| GLTF-175 | `tangent.w` handedness end-to-end | ✅ | GLTF-169 | Importer → stride 48/68 → `vBitangentSign` → `B = cross(N,T)*sign`. **Accept:** `tangent-handedness` renders the two halves of a normal-mapped quad with opposite, correct lighting at L7. **Complete first on EasyGL and now source-locked across every PBR renderer.** `tangent-handedness` is one mesh with two primitives carrying the same `N=+Z`, `T=+X`, UV frame, material and constant tangent-space approximately-`+Y` normal map, but opposite authored `w`. L3 asserts both signs, L4 reconstructs `B=+Y/-Y`, L5 locks the two independent stride-48 streams byte-for-byte, and the direct production-path test proves the importer did not default either sign. Registered `EasyGL_Gltf_TangentHandedness` closes the framebuffer half through `ContentManager` and `Model::Draw`: it obtains analytic byte **151** for `w=+1` versus exact **0** for `w=-1` on OPENGLES2 and OPENGLES3. Dropping the sign makes both bright, ignoring the normal map makes both black, and inverting it swaps them. `GLTF-379` now additionally requires every rigid and skinned PBR shader to carry that local sign together with the transform determinants described by `GLTF-176`; this does not claim fixture-driven L7 on every backend. |
| GLTF-176 | Normal/tangent transformation under node transforms | ✅ | GLTF-118, GLTF-119 | Only relevant if `GLTF-103` chooses baking; the shader's `uNormalMatrix` needs it either way. **Accept:** `xf-scale-nonuniform` and `tangent-mirrored` correct at L4/L7. **Complete first on EasyGL, then extended to all 15 PBR paths by `GLTF-379`.** The normal half remains `normal-nonuniform-scale`: exact `Rz90*S(2,3,4)` plus a slanted normal separates inverse transpose from both raw world 3×3 and an accidental transpose at L4/L6. `tangent-mirrored` places one shared, normal-mapped `(+X,+1)` tangent buffer normally and through `T(3,0,0)*S(-1,1,1)`; L4 states that both placements reconstruct world `B=+Y`, while L5 proves the local buffer remains unchanged. Before the EasyGL fix, its registered witness rendered byte **151** for the ordinary placement and exact **0** for the mirrored one. Its two PBR programs multiply `tangent.w` by World, optional-instance and (when skinned) blended-skin determinant signs; singular transforms retain the authored sign. The cross-renderer audit found every other PBR implementation still forwarded raw `w`. All now apply World and, for skinning, palette signs; Magnum includes its optional instance matrix, and Metal's skinned tangent also gains its missing World direction transform. Vulkan independently mirrors World and one joint in separate cases: both render byte **79**, while either old path is black; its focused executable is 8/8. `EveryPbrShaderComposesDirectionDeterminantsIntoTangentHandedness` locks both variants across all 15 implementations without claiming other renderers' stock effects or corpus-wide L7. |
| GLTF-177 | Normal renormalisation policy | ✅ | GLTF-173 | **Accept:** the seven points are tabulated in `docs/gltf-conventions.md` — which stage breaks unit length and where it is restored — and the two that are CNA's own are tested. `GltfMorphBlending.ABlendedNormalAndTangentComeBackUnitLength`: a weighted sum of unit normals is not unit, and the shortfall is largest exactly where morphing is most visible (two normals 90° apart at half weight sum to 0.707, so a fully morphed surface lights ~30% too dark). The shader's `normalize` would rescue the *drawn* result but not a CPU reader of the blended buffer, which is why the renormalisation is on the buffer everything sees — and it must not touch `TANGENT.w`, which is a sign, not a length. `GltfUvChannel.AnAuthoredNonUnitNormalSurvivesImportUnchanged`: import does **not** renormalise, because that would hide a broken export and make the vertex bytes disagree with the accessor the L5 goldens compare them to. |
| GLTF-178 | Normal/tangent L3 manifests for the corpus | ✅ | GLTF-101 | **Accept:** normals were already compared component-wise over the whole corpus; tangents now are too, through a new `"tangents"` field in the L3 record and a new `mat-authored-tangent` fixture — the corpus had no primitive authoring `TANGENT` at all, so the comparison would have had nothing to compare. The fixture's handedness is **−1**, not the +1 a generator falls back to, so "the authored basis was dropped and regenerated" shows up in the sign alone. **Only authored tangents are stated:** a generated basis is CNA's own algorithm rather than anything §3.7.2.1 prescribes, and putting one in the manifest would promote an implementation choice to a conformance requirement — the one thing this corpus must not do. |
| GLTF-179 | MikkTSpace parity investigation | ✔ | GLTF-170 | Measure the divergence from reference MikkTSpace on a seam-heavy fixture. **Accept:** the error is quantified and either accepted with numbers or scheduled. **Measured against the unmodified reference and accepted as the current fallback boundary.** Upstream commit `3e895b49d05ea07e4c2133156cfa94369e19e409`, `mikktspace.c` SHA-256 `de87e74107df766ce68108801262bd8d53899414236b59810509a8fc2a51e288`, `genTangSpaceDefault`. The witness is two triangles meeting along a data-compatible but separately indexed edge: CNA keeps their `(+X)` and `normalize(1,10,0)` per-index bases, while MikkTSpace welds the four edge corners to `(0.741453,0.671005,0)`. Across all six corners the angular difference is **34.4110° RMS, 42.1447° maximum**, with **0/6 handedness mismatches**. `GeneratedTangentsHaveAQuantifiedMikkTSpaceWeldDivergence` locks the production output against the six reference vectors and both aggregates. This is not a universal bound. Full parity needs MikkTSpace's per-corner/unindexed result, vertex splitting, rewriting every per-vertex stream and re-indexing; its own API says not to average into an existing index list. Accepted because generation is the fallback only — authored `TANGENT` remains byte-exact. Full method and disposition: `docs/gltf-conformance.md` §6. |
| GLTF-180 | Document the tangent algorithm | ✅ | GLTF-179 | **Accept:** `docs/gltf-conformance.md` §6 states the algorithm — the per-triangle U-gradient, **angle-weighted** accumulation onto each corner, Gram-Schmidt against the vertex normal, handedness from `sign(dot(cross(N,T), B))` — and its measured boundary. This is **not** MikkTSpace, which most authoring tools bake normal maps against: `GLTF-179` now quantifies 34.4110° RMS / 42.1447° maximum on a compatible duplicated-edge witness, rather than naming an unmeasured hard-UV-seam example that did not accurately describe the reference's grouping rule. Three consequences are explicit: authored `TANGENT` always wins and passes through byte-exact, a degenerate UV triangle contributes nothing rather than a NaN, and full parity requires per-corner topology expansion rather than averaging back into today's indices. |

### Phase 9 — UV sets and texture transform · owner: `GLTF-TEXTURE`
*Entry: Phase 8. Exit: ≥2 real UV channels, or an explicit, reported limitation.*

| ID | Title | St | Deps | Scope, evidence → acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GLTF-181 | **Decide: real second UV channel vs documented single-channel limit** | ✔ | GLTF-025 | **Accept:** decision recorded with its vertex-stride and shader cost. **The original single-channel decision was reversed by the final CORE audit** (`docs/gltf-api-change-review.md` §1.9): §27.1 row 7 requires simultaneous `TEXCOORD_0/1`, so a named wrong sample cannot satisfy it. The cost analysis remains the basis of the adopted ABI. A natural rigid stride 56 collides with skinned+colour, so rigid PBR keeps its complete 48-byte prefix, appends UV1 at byte 48 and four deterministic padding bytes for stride **60**; skinned PBR keeps its 68-byte prefix and appends UV1 for stride **76**. Five map selectors and renderer variants pay the shader cost without changing legacy 48/68 behaviour. |
| GLTF-182 | Second UV channel in the vertex layout (if adopted) | ✅ | GLTF-181, GLTF-155 | **Complete.** Strides 60/76 carry two disjoint sampled authored sets with byte-compatible legacy prefixes and deterministic rigid padding. L3/L5 lock both streams, direct and offline loaders agree, EasyGL OPENGLES2/3 and Vulkan render `uv1-material` correctly, both full PBR L7 corpora remain zero-delta, and the pinned Khronos cross-check independently passes. Vulkan's first retake caught that its PBR pipelines still hard-coded 48/68; `66a7a3131` passes the real draw stride into distinct 48/60/68/76 pipeline-cache variants and binds UV1 at byte 48/68. SOFTWARE now accepts the same canonical strides and consumes the base-colour selector in its deliberately reduced PBR fallback; its own corpus capture renders the complete 45,643-pixel quad. **Closing commits:** `9bab25e10`, `4a52c6000`, `b3a0404de`, `88b9c8da0`, `66a7a3131`, `305201296`. |
| GLTF-183 | Per-map `texCoord` selection reaches the shader | ✅ | GLTF-182 | **Complete.** Identical public five-slot selectors on `PbrEffect` and `SkinnedPbrEffect` survive direct glTF and offline `.cnj`, become one `GpuDrawParams::pbrTextureCoordinateSetMask`, and drive independent base-colour/normal/MR/emissive/occlusion selection. L6 parity and rigid/skinned EasyGL framebuffer controls prove the property is consumed rather than merely carried. Vulkan now has separate dual-UV SPIR-V variants which consume the same five-bit mask while leaving its legacy single-UV shader blobs bit-identical; `uv1-material` increased from the broken half-quad 19,468 foreground pixels to the complete 45,644-pixel quad in two byte-identical processes. SOFTWARE consumes bit 0 for the only PBR map its CPU fallback samples and now reaches the same fixture through stride 60/76 without claiming the other four maps. **Closing commits:** `5e1131ddb`, `32dff1a85`, `31f9188fe`, `b3a0404de`, `88b9c8da0`, `66a7a3131`, `305201296`. |
| GLTF-184 | `KHR_texture_transform` per map, not just base colour | ✅ | GLTF-183 | **Complete.** `MaterialOut`, `PbrEffect` and `SkinnedPbrEffect` retain five authored transforms; `GpuDrawParams` carries ten affine rows and every full PBR renderer consumes them. Authored UV bytes remain unchanged, direct glTF and optional `.cnj` state agree at L6, and the old bake plus `unbakedTextureTransformsEXT` diagnostic are gone. `texture-transform-per-map` uses one UV stream with deliberately different base/normal matrices; registered `EasyGL_Gltf_TextureTransformPerMap` renders the expected blue base sample and +Z normal sample on OPENGLES2 and OPENGLES3. The post-rollout EasyGL and Vulkan production-viewer retakes are green for all 145 assets. SOFTWARE independently applies slot zero's affine rows in its base-colour-only fallback and produces deterministic transform captures, without being counted as evidence for the four PBR maps it intentionally does not shade. **Closing commits:** `ad8958c5a`, `9ada653f6`, `474c6b223`, `629dbfba8`, `305201296`. |
| GLTF-185 | `KHR_texture_transform` maths lock | ✔ | GLTF-184 | Scale → rotate → translate, matching the spec's reference formula; `ExtractMeshAppliesTextureTransformAndEmissiveStrength` exists. **Accept:** extended with a rotation-bearing fixture. **Locked with parameters no two orderings agree on**: a non-uniform scale, a quarter turn, and an asymmetric offset. Any other order gives a different image from the same three numbers, which is the entire content of the rule. An identity transform is asserted to be an exact no-op — otherwise every material that merely *mentions* the extension shifts its textures. |
| GLTF-186 | Baked vs shader-side texture transform decision | ✅ | GLTF-184 | **Executed decision, documented in `docs/gltf-conventions.md`: per-map transforms are shader-side.** Baking was the only practical choice while CNA carried one sampled stream. `GLTF-182`/`183` supplied dual streams and selectors; `GLTF-184` completed five per-map transforms and retired destructive baking plus `unbakedTextureTransformsEXT` as one coordinated change. |
| GLTF-187 | `KHR_texture_transform.texcoord` override | ✔ | GLTF-185 | Already honoured for base colour. **Accept:** locked, and extended per map. **Locked with the view and the transform deliberately disagreeing** — the view says set 0, the transform says set 1 — because agreement is the case that cannot distinguish them. An implementation reading only the view bakes exactly the wrong set and still produces a fully textured mesh. |
| GLTF-188 | Retire or repurpose `pbrUv2Mismatch` | ✅ | GLTF-184 | **Narrowed, not removed.** `pbrUv2Mismatch` first became the actionable map-name list `uvSetMismatchedMapsEXT`; after `GLTF-182`/`183`, it fires only when a material samples a **third distinct authored UV set** beyond the two packed channels. A texture CNA cannot decode is excluded because its problem is the missing image, not UV selection. Both loaders and the offline tool emit the same third-set wording and tests assert the affected names. **Closing diagnostic correction:** `25b582534`. |
| GLTF-189 | UV wrap behaviour with out-of-range coordinates | ✔ | GLTF-203 | `uv-out-of-range-clamp/wrap/mirror`. **Accept:** each matches its sampler at L7. **Landed on all three committed fixtures and both EasyGL profiles.** They share the exact 16×16 asymmetric image, quad and authored UVs; only the sampler differs. At source position `(13/15,14/15)` the interpolated UV is `(1.05,1.6)`: CLAMP resolves to the bottom-right yellow `(230,190,20)`, REPEAT to bottom-left blue `(40,70,220)`, and MIRRORED_REPEAT to top-right green `(30,160,60)`. The point is strictly inside the quad and every resolved coordinate lies inside a flat quadrant, away from both boundaries and numeral glyphs, so linear filtering cannot make a plausible near-edge blend. `EasyGL_Gltf_SamplerWrap` loads each `.gltf` through `ContentManager`, asserts the part carries `Linear` plus the expected U/V mode, applies that public per-slot state to device slot zero, then renders the real `PbrEffect` through `Model::Draw`; all three exact authored colours pass within 3 on OPENGLES2 and OPENGLES3. Applying the state remains explicitly application-owned per `GLTF-208`; the test exercises that documented CNAEXT boundary rather than making `Model::Draw` silently overwrite global device state. |
| GLTF-190 | Asymmetric checkerboard reference texture | ✅ | GLTF-009 | **Accept:** `tools/gltf_fixtures/png.py` — a 16×16 image with a distinct saturated colour **and** a distinct numeral per quadrant, so a V flip, a UV-set swap and a 180° rotation are each individually visible (a plain checkerboard is symmetric under all three, which is why they keep shipping). The numerals are 3×5 glyphs drawn in the generator rather than rendered, because a font would be both a dependency and a version the committed bytes depend on. It required a **PNG encoder**, written against `zlib`/`struct` alone — the corpus had no textured asset at all, which is what blocked every texture-shaped row from having a fixture behind its unit test. `tex-reference-checkerboard` carries it on a quad that samples all four quadrants, with a declared `NEAREST`/`CLAMP_TO_EDGE` sampler, and the flip is detectable at **L3** from the decoded UVs. |
| GLTF-191 | UV origin invariant | ✔ | GLTF-105 | Both glTF and XNA use top-left. **Accept:** asserted; no flip anywhere in the importer. **Asserted with `GLTF-105`**, on authored UVs that are asymmetric in V (0.75, 0.5, 0.0625) so a mirror cannot land back on itself. This is also the assertion `GLTF-192`'s render-target V-flip must not be allowed to leak into: that flip belongs to the sampler at draw time, not to the asset's own coordinates. |
| GLTF-192 | Render-target V-flip must not leak into asset UVs | ✔ | GLTF-191 | `cnaSampleUV(vUV, uRtFlipV.*)` exists for render targets. **Accept:** a fixture proves a non-render-target draw is unaffected. **Closed by the two-sided lock now documented in `docs/gltf-conventions.md`.** `GltfConventions.PositionsNormalsAndUvsPassThroughUnchanged` keeps asymmetric asset V values byte-exact, and `tex-reference-checkerboard` makes a mirror visible in every quadrant. At the renderer boundary, the shared non-square 8x4 orientation fixture uses standard unflipped mesh UVs and requires an ordinary `Texture2D` to render upright before comparing an equivalent `RenderTarget2D`; its CD1/CD2 non-indexed/indexed BasicEffect legs are the direct non-render-target control this row required. EasyGL's flags start at zero on every draw, are set only by `SampledRowOrderIsBottomUp` for each bound source, and are uploaded unconditionally even when zero, so a prior target cannot leak a stale flip into a glTF texture. The full oracle is EasyGL 62/62, Vulkan 58/58 and SOFTWARE 62/62; HEADLESS passes its explicit no-raster/refusal boundary. |
