### Phase 18 — Effect and shader boundary · owner: `CNA-EFFECT`
*Entry: Phase 11 + Phase 12. Exit: every §21.1 row is asserted numerically at L6.*

| ID | Title | St | Deps | Scope, evidence → acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GLTF-365 | L6 capture for every stock effect | ✔ | GLTF-008 | **Accept:** `BasicEffect`, `DualTextureEffect`, `SkinnedEffect`, `PbrEffect`, `SkinnedPbrEffect` all capturable. **Locked by `EveryStockEffectIsCapturableWithItsOwnDistinguishingParameter`.** The capture reads through `Effect::FillGpuDrawParams`, the same virtual call the draw path makes, so being effect-agnostic is a property of the design — but a capture that quietly produced an empty record for an unfamiliar effect would narrow every later "asserted at L6" claim to `PbrEffect` alone, which is why it is asserted rather than argued. Two of the five are unreachable by import (glTF has no dual-texture material, and a skinned glTF primitive selects `SkinnedPbrEffect` or `SkinnedEffect` by its material model), so they are bound onto a real imported part — same buffers, same primitive count, only the effect changes — instead of a fixture pretending to produce one. Each is identified by the parameter only it sets, which is why `DrawParamsDump` grew a `dualTexture` field: "capturable" means nothing if the record cannot tell the effects apart. |
| GLTF-366 | Matrices agree end-to-end | ✔ | GLTF-266 | **Accept:** L6. **Locked by three L6 tests together**: `BoundWorldMatrixMatchesTheExpectedNodeWorld` (against `L4`'s own stated world, not a recomputation), `ViewAndProjectionReachEveryDrawUnaltered`, and `NormalMatrixIsTheInverseTransposeOfTheWorldUpper3x3` — with `NonUniformScaleSeparatesTheNormalMatrixFromTheWorldMatrix` as the case that makes the third discriminating at all, since under uniform scale the normal matrix and the world matrix agree. |
| GLTF-367 | Normal matrix under non-uniform scale | ✔ | GLTF-267 | **Accept:** L6 value equals `transpose(inverse(world3x3))`. **Locked by `NonUniformScaleSeparatesTheNormalMatrixFromTheWorldMatrix`** on `xf-scale-nonuniform`: under `scale = [2,3,4]` the captured `uNormalMatrix` is `diag(1/2, 1/3, 1/4)`, and the test additionally sends the fixture's own `(0.6, 0.8, 0)` normal through both derivations and requires the two results to point measurably apart — without that, a normal matrix that had quietly become the world 3×3 would pass, since the two agree under every uniform scale. The corpus-wide half is `NormalMatrixIsTheInverseTransposeOfTheWorldUpper3x3`, which compares against XNA's own `Matrix::Invert` and counts asymmetric worlds, failing if it sees none. |
| GLTF-368 | `DiffuseColor` / `Alpha` carry `baseColorFactor` | ✔ | GLTF-216 | **Accept:** L6. **Locked by `MaterialFactorsReachTheBoundEffect`**, which compares all four components of every fixture's manifest `baseColorFactor` against the captured `diffuseColor` — including the alpha in `[3]`, which is the component D7 lost and the one a premultiplying effect would corrupt (`GLTF-369` is the reason `PbrEffect` deliberately does not). Swept over every loadable fixture with a floor assertion, so a sweep that stopped finding materials fails instead of passing quietly. |
| GLTF-369 | `PbrEffect` does not premultiply alpha into RGB | ✅ | GLTF-368 | Deliberate and correct for glTF. **Accept:** locked with a comment-backed test. **Locked by `EasyGL_Gltf_AlphaBlend` on both EasyGL profiles.** The imported source is `(1,.72,.315,.5)` and the test first observes the full straight RGB under `Opaque`. Over blue, `NonPremultiplied` then produces `(128,92,168)`, while premultiplied `AlphaBlend` produces `(255,184,208)` because it consumes the full source RGB. Premultiplying inside the fragment program would instead make the two blend presets coincide at the former result, so the three-way framebuffer oracle observes exactly the shader-side property a CPU parameter capture cannot. |
| GLTF-370 | Metallic / roughness / emissive parameters | ✔ | GLTF-220 | **Accept:** L6. **Locked by the same sweep**: `pbrMetallicFactor`, `pbrRoughnessFactor` and `emissiveColor` against the manifest's own values, on every fixture that carries a material. The emissive comparison reads `emissiveFactorTimesStrength` where a fixture carries `KHR_materials_emissive_strength`, so a strength applied twice or not at all is a mismatch against a stated number rather than against a value the test recomputed. |
| GLTF-371 | Normal scale and occlusion strength parameters | ✔ | GLTF-224, GLTF-225 | **Accept:** L6. **Locked by `NormalScaleAndOcclusionStrengthReachTheBoundEffect`** on `mat-normal-occlusion-scale`, whose 0.35 and 0.8 are authored away from 1 and away from each other so that a dropped value and a swapped pair are different failures, with `AMaterialDeclaringNeitherScalarGetsGltfsOwnDefaultOfOne` as the control that an undeclared scalar gets glTF's own 1 rather than whatever the previous material left on the effect. |
| GLTF-372 | Alpha-test parameters actually configured | ✔ | GLTF-229 | `uAlphaTest` exists but nothing sets it. **Accept:** L6 shows the cutoff. **Fixed at the effect boundary, and it was a live silent loss:** neither `PbrEffect` nor `SkinnedPbrEffect` filled the vector in, so a `MASK` material rendered exactly like an `OPAQUE` one at L6. Both effects now derive it through one mapping (`AlphaTestVectorForAlphaModeEXT`, `CNA/Internal/Graphics/AlphaCoverageEXT.hpp`) rather than two hand-written copies of a four-element sign convention in which swapping `z`/`w` inverts the cutout. `MASK` is `{cutoff, 0, -1, +1}`; `OPAQUE` and `BLEND` keep the never-discard `{0,0,1,1}` default, because what they need is `BlendState` and a draw order the application owns — `GLTF-230`, and `docs/gltf-api-change-review.md` §1.3 now records where that line falls. The new fixture `mat-alpha-mask-cutoff` authors a cutoff of **0.75** — neither glTF's 0.5 default nor the material's own 0.875 alpha — so a cutoff that never arrived and one wired to the wrong alpha quantity are both visible as values; `AMaskMaterialsCutoffReachesTheDrawsAlphaTestVector` compares against the manifest's stated `gpuAlphaTest` and then evaluates the shader's own discard expression just below, exactly at and well above the cutoff, so the sign convention is asserted as behaviour. `OnlyAMaskMaterialWritesAnAlphaTestReference` is the corpus-wide control. The later `GLTF-379` renderer audit corrected an over-broad original conclusion: eight PBR renderer implementations did **not** consume this now-correct vector, and five routed PBR+MASK through a generic alpha-test effect. That distinct L7/backend defect is now fixed and guarded there rather than retroactively attributed to this L6 task. |
| GLTF-373 | Texture slot → uniform mapping | ✅ | GLTF-365 | **Accept:** each of the five maps binds to its intended unit on every renderer. **Locked across all 15 PBR implementations, without pretending unlike native APIs share one numeric ABI.** `GltfRendererPbrFallbackPolicy.EveryPbrMapReachesTheShaderBindingIntendedByItsRenderer` checks the complete CPU field → native binding → shader declaration chain for Bgfx, Diligent, D3D9/11/12, EasyGL, LLGL, Magnum, Metal, OpenGL2/4, SDL GPU, Vulkan, WebGPU and Wicked; its inventory test makes a newly added PBR renderer fail until represented. The ABI is `[base, normal, metallic-roughness, emissive, occlusion] = 0..4` where that API exposes ordinary units, LLGL uses texture bindings `2/4/6/8/10`, WebGPU group 1 uses `1..5` after its sampler, and Wicked deliberately uses `t0/t3/t4/t5/t6` around the legacy second-texture/cube slots. A second test reads separately stored rigid/skinned shader sources independently so one variant cannot drift behind an aggregate match. `EasyGL_Pbr_TextureSlots` and `Vulkan_Pbr_TextureSlots` then render **10 discriminating cases each** (five semantic sentinel maps × rigid/skinned): red base, green MR (`G=1,B=0`), blue emissive, asymmetric occlusion and a tilted normal. The expected linear bytes are analytic and every case passes on real EasyGL and Vulkan/llvmpipe. `EasyGL_Pbr_MaterialMaps` additionally exercises all five bindings under EasyGL's output-sRGB and scalar extensions. Exact matrix and evidence boundary: `docs/gltf-renderer-pbr-fallbacks.md`. |
| GLTF-374 | Unbound-map fallbacks | ✅ | GLTF-373 | EasyGL has a flat-normal fallback (`CNB-58`) and white for the rest — correct. **Accept:** every PBR-capable renderer has the same fallbacks; a missing flat-normal fallback tilts every normal and must be caught. **Audited and made executable across all 15 PBR implementations.** `GltfRendererPbrFallbackPolicy` discovers every renderer directory that consumes `pbrNormalMap` and requires the inventory to equal Bgfx, Diligent, D3D9/11/12, EasyGL, LLGL, Magnum, Metal, OpenGL2/4, SDL GPU, Vulkan, WebGPU and Wicked. For each, it locks the actual map→fallback pairing and the texels: normal→RGBA8 `(128,128,255,255)`, every other map→white. White is a catastrophic normal default, not a small colour error: decoded/normalised `(1,1,1)` is 54.7° from the geometric normal, while the conventional 8-bit flat texel is about 0.32° away (zero is not exactly representable after unsigned-normalised `*2-1`). `docs/gltf-renderer-pbr-fallbacks.md` records runtime evidence per renderer and distinguishes the D3D12/Wicked/Metal hardware gates from the source-policy proof. Existing analytic/golden PBR pixel tests exercise null optional maps on the runnable backends; the new ordinary `CnaTests` audit means an unavailable backend can no longer lose the fallback silently. |
| GLTF-375 | Bone palette and influence count | ✔ | GLTF-263 | **Accept:** L6. **Locked by `SkinnedDrawBindsThePaletteAndFourInfluencesPerVertex`**, which compares the bound palette against `ModelMesh::GetSkinTransforms()` rather than a literal — the palette is what the application would read, so the two must be the same object's view of one thing. |
| GLTF-376 | Light parameters | ✔ | GLTF-325 | **Accept:** L6 shows disabled lights as zero colour, matching the shader's expectation. **Locked, and the harness had to grow to do it** — `DrawParamsDump` now captures all three `light*Dir`/`light*Diffuse` pairs. The shape being pinned is XNA's, not glTF's: three directional lights with a **disabled** one expressed as a zero diffuse colour rather than by a flag, because the shaders add all three unconditionally. Asserted over every loadable fixture: each channel finite and within `[0,1]` (a negative one *subtracts* light, which nothing later checks for) and each direction unit length *even when the light contributes nothing*, since a zero-length direction normalises to NaN in any shader that normalises defensively. A second test pins the property that makes the path safe by construction — a file declaring no lights, which is the common case, must still receive a light that actually contributes, or a lit effect renders black. |
| GLTF-377 | Fog parameters do not leak into glTF draws | ✔ | GLTF-365 | `vFogFactor` is in the PBR shader. **Accept:** fog off by default; asserted. **Locked by `NoImportedDrawArrivesWithFogEnabled`** over every loadable fixture, on both `fogEnabled` and the four-component `fogVector` — the two can disagree, and a renderer that ignored the flag would still fog on a non-zero vector, so the all-zero vector (`dot(position, 0) = 0`, hence `keep = 1`) is the statement that actually holds whatever a renderer does with the flag. `TheFogCaptureCanActuallySeeFog` is the control: enabling fog by hand, as an XNA application may, must move both quantities — without it the sweep would be satisfied by a capture that could not see fog at all. Fog is state no glTF file can request, which is what makes any non-zero value here shading that came from nowhere. |
| GLTF-378 | Shader source review against the spec BRDF | ✅ | GLTF-235 | Matches Appendix B. **Accept:** documented, with the deliberate `roughness` clamp to `[0.045,1]` noted. |
| GLTF-379 | Cross-renderer shader semantic audit | ✅ | GLTF-373 | **Accept:** a per-renderer matrix of the §21.1 rows. **All eleven slices complete.** Alpha coverage: the source inventory found eight PBR implementations that did not consume `GpuDrawParams::alphaTest`, plus five renderers that gave their generic alpha-test effect priority over PBR and therefore lost the PBR material/layout; all are corrected and PBR wins dispatch wherever the paths competed. Map scalars: EasyGL alone consumed `normalTexture.scale` and `occlusionTexture.strength`; the other 14 renderers now upload both values and implement the exact XY-only normal scaling and `1 + strength * (sampled - 1)` equations. Skinned normals: EasyGL alone inverse-transposed a non-uniform joint/palette matrix; all other skinned PBR paths now do the same, Metal gains its missing world normal matrix, OpenGL2 uses `uNormalMatrix`, and WebGPU's rigid/skinned PBR bindings advertise their full size. Tangent orientation: EasyGL alone folded direction-transform determinant signs into `tangent.w`; all 15 rigid/skinned PBR variants now cover World and blended skin, the two instance-aware PBR implementations cover their optional instance matrix, and Metal's skinned tangent gains its missing World direction transform. Colour transfer: EasyGL alone consumed the L6 base/emissive/output declarations; every PBR backend now decodes the two sRGB texture roles, leaves factors and the other three maps linear, mixes fog in linear space and optionally encodes final RGB. Packed channels: all 15 already used normal RGB with `*2−1`, MR green→roughness, MR blue→metallic and occlusion red; the missing piece was an inventory-wide regression gate, now added with exact copy counts for separate rigid/skinned shaders and LLGL's generated header. Fresnel endpoints: all 15 rigid/skinned PBR paths now upload imported F0/F90, mix dielectric and metal endpoints separately and evaluate Schlick with `(F90-F0)` rather than reconstructing a constant grazing endpoint. Core material factors: the 15-renderer matrix requires each rigid/skinned fragment to multiply decoded base RGB and alpha by their independent factors, emissive by its own factor, and MR G/B by roughness/metallic; LLGL's generated header is counted separately. Skin palette/influence count: every backend now has an exact PBR CPU palette/count upload and shader-side 1/2/4 gating row, paired with the PBR-specific inverse-transpose audit so a generic skin shader cannot satisfy it accidentally. Transform transport: every backend now has an exact CPU `World * View * Projection` composition/upload row, retains World independently for shading, and consumes the combined transform in both rigid and post-skin PBR clip-position paths; inline WebGPU owners are scoped separately to prevent a generic WGSL false positive. Caller-owned double-sided state: every backend maps `CullMode::None` to native no-cull and its rigid/skinned PBR path consumes the same dynamic state or immutable pipeline key; Vulkan and WebGPU owners are scoped separately, while EasyGL supplies the discriminating imported-property back-face L7 witness. One source inventory suite covers all 15 implementations and every separately stored variant. Vulkan retains the 18 texture/scalar/MASK pixels and adds the exact 12-case colour matrix; the focused executable is 8/8 for joint inverse-transpose plus mirrored World/joint TBN. The same ten-case packed-channel sentinel passes on OPENGLES3 and Vulkan, satisfying `GLTF-234`; shared mid-grey colour and six-check Fresnel tests are registered across the runnable backends, with explicit compiler/build-only caveats for unavailable D3D and Metal runtime environments. The affected shader generators and native/cross targets compile, Wicked passes its runtime colour/Fresnel checks on real Vulkan, and `docs/gltf-renderer-pbr-fallbacks.md` carries all eleven exact matrices. The §21.1 acceptance matrix is complete. |
| GLTF-380 | Effect parameter naming vs convention | ✔ | GLTF-379 | "A field with the same name is not the same convention until a test says so." **Accept:** every shared name has a test. **Locked for the duplicated rigid/skinned PBR surface by `RigidAndSkinnedPbrEffectsGiveEverySharedNameTheSameConvention`.** One generic configurator must compile against both public APIs and authors asymmetric World/View/Projection, base/ambient/emissive colours, alpha/MASK cutoff, all five maps, metallic/roughness, IOR/specular F0/F90, normal/occlusion scalars, three independent colour-space flags, three lights (including a disabled middle light) and non-default fog. The resulting `GpuDrawParams` blocks are compared field by field across every shared texture, material, transform, light and fog name. Only `skinned`, the bone palette/count and `weightsPerVertex` may differ. The same test passes on STUB, HEADLESS and real OPENGLES3, so neither duplicated setter spelling nor a renderer happens to hide a convention mismatch. |
| GLTF-381 | `ShaderEffect` interaction | ✔ | GLTF-365 | A custom `ShaderEffect` must still receive correct glTF vertex data. **Accept:** a fixture with a custom shader over a glTF mesh. **Locked without inventing an L7 result.** `GltfDrawParamsOracleL6.ACustomShaderKeepsTheImportedTangentVertexContract` loads the existing `mat-authored-tangent` corpus witness, replaces its imported `PbrEffect` with a real `ShaderEffect`, and keeps the original `ModelMeshPart`, vertex/index buffers, topology and primitive count. The shader declares POSITION/NORMAL/TANGENT/TEXCOORD at locations 0–3, exactly the stride-48 element order. Before the draw, all four streams are read back from the part's actual vertex buffer and compared to the fixture's independently generated L3 record; the deliberately authored `TANGENT.w = -1` distinguishes preserved handedness from the common generated/default +1. `FillGpuDrawParams` is then required to select the custom-program renderer rather than a stock PBR path. On `HEADLESS`, where `ThreeD`, `CustomEffects` and a valid effect renderer all exist, the test executes the complete `Model::Draw` route, proves World/View/Projection reached the custom effect, and confirms the vertex bytes are unchanged afterwards. STUB runs the data/effect-replacement half and deliberately makes no draw claim because its capability is false. This is L6 evidence about the custom-effect boundary, not a pixel claim; actual shader attribute rasterization remains in the renderer-specific L7 matrix rather than being smuggled into this row. |
| GLTF-382 | Effect-boundary regression over the corpus | ✔ | GLTF-365 | **Accept:** L6 green for every fixture on `HEADLESS`. **40 L6 tests green on `HEADLESS`** (`GltfConformanceL6`, `GltfDrawParamsOracleL6`, `GltfLightingPolicy`, `GltfLightBudget`), which is the same set that is green on `STUB` — and most of those tests are themselves corpus-wide sweeps rather than single fixtures, so "every fixture" is covered by the sweeps' own floors rather than by counting. Worth stating plainly: this is the first row in Phase 18 that needed a renderer other than `STUB`, and the second renderer found nothing at L6. What it did find was one layer up — see `GLTF-383`. |

### Phase 19 — Renderer differential validation · owner: `CNA-RENDERER`

| GLTF-392 | **Forbid per-renderer fixes for shared defects** | ✔ | GLTF-391 | **Accept:** a review rule; a shared-importer fix appearing in a renderer directory fails review. **Written *and* enforced, because this is the rule most likely to be broken by someone acting in good faith.** The wrongness is visible on one renderer, the renderer is open in front of you, and two lines make the picture right — at which point the importer's mistake is *compensated* on one of N renderers, the other N−1 stay wrong, the compensation is invisible to L1–L6 (which never look at a renderer), and the next person to fix the importer properly breaks the renderer that was compensating. `docs/gltf-conventions.md` states that; `GltfSharedDefectPolicy` checks the half a compiler can see — **no renderer source may include the glTF importer** (so a renderer cannot make an import decision even if someone wants it to), and **the importer may include `Renderers/Common/` and no renderer implementation** (so "correct" cannot quietly come to mean "correct on EasyGL", which is what makes `GLTF-017`'s independence claim checkable at all). `scripts/gltf-renderer-parity.sh` is the other half: any L1–L5 difference between two renderers fails, and its message names this rule. |

The audit found and fixed a real Vulkan gap: `PointListEXT` previously fell through to triangle topology and a zero vertex count. Vulkan now maps it to `VK_PRIMITIVE_TOPOLOGY_POINT_LIST`; every 3D vertex shader writes the required one-pixel `gl_PointSize`, and regenerated SPIR-V carries it. `PointListPrimitiveTest` now runs 15/15 on the real Vulkan device — non/indexed, 32-bit, dynamic/user draws, exact ranges, topology switches, target/depth/cull/viewport/scissor/blend plus the actual stride-48 PBR point pipeline — and every case requires zero validation messages. The complete Vulkan `*Gltf*` selection remains 520/520. |
| GLTF-394 | Unsupported-feature behaviour per renderer | ✔ | GLTF-393 | **Accept:** a clear "not supported" message, never a silent wrong draw. **Closed at both kinds of renderer boundary exposed by `GLTF-393`.** In the four campaign arms, STUB advertises `ThreeD=false` and every draw-requiring glTF test skips with that capability named; HEADLESS records topology/upload state without claiming pixels; OPENGLES3 and Vulkan support every listed core draw feature. The shared 32-bit-index default now throws the exact width-specific error locked by `GLTF-163`. The audit also followed `GLTF-386`'s Direct3D comparison route and found the last known silent `POINTS` reinterpretations: D3D9 had five separate topology mappers and D3D10/D3D11 each had a count and native mapper that inherited/fell back to triangle behaviour. They now map `PointListEXT` to `D3DPT_POINTLIST`, `D3D10_PRIMITIVE_TOPOLOGY_POINTLIST` and `D3D11_PRIMITIVE_TOPOLOGY_POINTLIST`, with D3D9/10/11 admitted to the shared framebuffer suite. D3D12 is deliberately different: its PSO cache still fixes `PrimitiveTopologyType` to triangle, so merely changing IA topology would be invalid; all four draw paths now reject `LineList`, `LineStrip` and `PointListEXT` at entry with `DirectX12 renderer does not support PrimitiveType::<name>` before target/layout/PSO checks can mask the reason. Unknown future enum values throw too. `GltfRendererPointTopologyPolicy.Direct3DBackendsMapPointsOrRejectBeforeSubmission` source-locks all five D3D9 copies, D3D10/11 count+mapping pairs and D3D12's four early guards. All four changed renderer libraries cross-compile with MinGW, and the shared point test cross-compiles for D3D9/10/11; no Windows framebuffer pass is claimed without an initialised dedicated Wine/DXVK prefix. |
| GLTF-395 | Winding and cull agreement across renderers | ✔ | GLTF-232 | **Accept:** the single-sided fixture culls the same face everywhere. **Closed at the renderer boundary where `GLTF-232` leaves the decision.** The shared `frontface_winding_test.cpp` pixel oracle authors complementary clockwise/counter-clockwise triangles and proves `CullNone`, `CullClockwiseFace` and XNA's default `CullCounterClockwiseFace` on the backbuffer, ordinary and 4x-MSAA targets, every primitive draw entry point, stock textured effects, determinant-preserving and mirrored worlds, depth/blend state, state transitions and SpriteBatch's own winding. EasyGL and Vulkan each pass **127/127 with zero skips** on real llvmpipe graphics devices; SOFTWARE passes the same suite, and HEADLESS passes its explicit non-rasterising/refusal boundary. SOFTWARE initially returned stale black for the active 4x target even though its cull result was correct: its rasteriser wrote the four-sample plane while `GetData` read the old resolved cache. Level-zero readback now snapshots the live samples without unbinding or exposing mip levels, and a focused `Software_RenderTargetReadback` H4 assertion locks that exact draw-before-unbind sequence. Thus every rasterising gate backend keeps the clockwise XNA front face and removes its counter-clockwise complement under the default state; negative determinant reverses the observed classification exactly once. |
| GLTF-396 | Depth-range and clip-space differences | ✔ | GLTF-395 | Owned by the renderer, not the importer. **Accept:** documented; the corpus is unaffected. **Closed with an explicit matrix in `docs/gltf-conventions.md`, not a claim that the native APIs agree.** CNA/XNA projection matrices emit Direct3D `0 <= z <= w`. Vulkan consumes that range natively and flips clip Y once in its stock shaders. EasyGL passes it into OpenGL ES's wider `-w <= z <= w` volume, so stock depth occupies window `[0.5,1]`: ordering/visibility remain monotonic, but half the depth interval is unused and negative-Z custom clip output is intentionally non-portable. SOFTWARE performs the XNA `MinDepth + (z/w)*(MaxDepth-MinDepth)` mapping exactly, but its CPU clipper cuts at `w>epsilon` (the eye plane) rather than all six homogeneous frustum planes. HEADLESS/STUB make no raster claim. The importer owns none of these operations: L5 positions and L6 matrices remain renderer-independent, and no Z/Y compensation was added there. Empirical guard: EasyGL and Vulkan each pass the shared **39/39** viewport suite including both `MinDepth/MaxDepth` ordering checks; SOFTWARE passes its **25/25** viewport suite and **4/4** depth contract; `*Gltf*` remains 520/520 on HEADLESS, OPENGLES3, Vulkan and SOFTWARE. The documented non-portable region is outside every current corpus camera/frustum draw, so “unaffected” is bounded evidence, not a promise about arbitrary custom clip-space shaders. |
| GLTF-397 | Render-target V-flip does not affect glTF UVs | ✔ | GLTF-192 | **Accept:** asserted per renderer. **Asserted on every campaign renderer that can make the distinction.** `rendertarget_sampling_orientation_test.cpp` authors no compensating V flip: NDC top-left maps to UV `(0,0)`, an 8x4 source is non-square and all 32 texels are unique, and each ordinary-texture control must itself be upright before a render target can pass by merely agreeing with it. EasyGL passes 62/62, including stock 3D Basic/AlphaTest/DualTexture sampling, target chains, public `SpriteEffects` flips, 4x MSAA, mips and state transitions; Vulkan passes 58/58 with its unsupported 4x path stated rather than fabricated; SOFTWARE passes 62/62 with real 4x CPU resolve; HEADLESS passes its deterministic refusal boundary. Combined with `GLTF-192`'s byte-exact importer UV lock and EasyGL PBR's five per-resource flip flags, this proves the renderer correction acts on render-target storage identity, never on glTF coordinates. |
| GLTF-398 | Renderer-differential CI job | ✔ | GLTF-390 | **Accept:** at least `STUB`, `HEADLESS` and `OPENGLES3` run per commit. **Closed and extended through EasyGL L7.** `.github/workflows/gltf-renderer-stride-ci.yml` triggers on every pushed branch and PR into `develop`/`master`; STUB, HEADLESS, OPENGLES3 and Vulkan each run all ten exact L0–L6/tool entries plus the focused seven-layout native boundary. A separate required job checks out viewer `f32d1f1`, builds it against the CNA commit with three compile jobs, and runs `CnaGltfConformanceL7` under Xvfb. Every job is fail-closed and the matrix keeps `fail-fast: false`. `RequiredCiRunsEveryCampaignRendererForEveryCommitAndCannotIgnoreFailure` locks the lower matrix; the pinned viewer hash and exact L7 test name make the image gate reviewable rather than following a mutable application branch. **Extension commit:** `70b26f629`. |

A fixture claiming `L4` with an empty `l4` is compared against nothing at that layer while the inventory reports coverage — worse than declaring nothing. Also asserted: the owning group is one of the counted groups, and a `referencingGroups` entry is a known group that is **not** the asset's own — naming its own would double-count it in exactly the way §24.1's ownership model exists to prevent. A floor over the corpus's declared layers keeps the sweep from shrinking. |
| GLTF-402 | Corpus runner reports the first divergent layer | ✔ | GLTF-010 | **Accept:** a failure names the layer, the fixture, the field and the delta. **All four, in that order, and `docs/gltf-conformance.md` §3.6 now shows two real failures rather than describing the property.** The **layer** is CTest's own result line, because each rung is its own entry registered lowest-first (`CnaGltfConformanceL5 ... Failed`); the **fixture** is the `SCOPED_TRACE` every sweep sets; the **field** and the **delta** are the assertion itself — `u8-idx VB differs at byte 45 (vertex 1, Normal +1): expected 0x00, actual 0xFF`. The layer comes first because it is the cheapest thing to act on: an L2 failure makes every value above it meaningless, and fixing the L5 golden of a fixture whose accessor decode is wrong wastes the afternoon. Higher rungs still run after a failure — whether a wrong world matrix also corrupted the bound effect parameters is worth knowing in the same run. |
| GLTF-403 | Corpus coverage matrix vs the §27.1 checklist | ✔ | GLTF-401 | **Accept:** every CORE requirement maps to ≥1 fixture. **§27.1.1 is that matrix**, one row per §27.1 requirement, and it is **checked rather than written**: `EverySection271RowIsTraceableToFixturesAndTestsThatExist` requires all 20 rows to be present and every fixture named to exist in the corpus. It failed on its first run against five fixture names that sounded right and were not (`anim-linear`, `attr-full-set`, `attr-ignored-custom`, `external-buffer-uri`, `bad-index-*`) — which is the whole argument for checking a coverage matrix instead of reading one. A wildcard (`skin-*`) is accepted as naming a family the sweeps cover; anything else must be a real asset. |

Local leak detection is disabled only because this environment denies its process inspection; CI keeps it on. Existing budgets remain unchanged (5/10/60/120/300 s) and leave about a 3× margin on the slower native steps. Enforcement is **per rung** (`TIMEOUT 300` on every `gltf-conformance` entry), so a hang names its layer rather than stalling the whole job. | GLTF-408 | Malformed-input group behaviour | ✔ | GLTF-040 | **Accept:** all 6 `bad-*` fixtures produce deterministic, actionable errors. **All seven now (the group grew with `GLTF-039` and `GLTF-068`), and "actionable" is asserted rather than asserted-to-be.** Every fixture declaring a `rejection` block is swept by `EveryFixtureDeclaringARejectionIsRejectedAndSaysWhy`, which requires three things: the file **parses** (a rejection fixture a parse-only reader would refuse proves nothing), it is refused at the **stage the manifest names** — validation and extraction are not interchangeable, and conflating them into "something threw" is how a check quietly stops running — and the diagnostic contains every fragment the manifest lists. Those fragments are the actionable part: `index 2 = 7` and `only 3 vertices`, not "an index is out of range"; `reads 36 bytes` and `bufferView of 24 bytes`, not "a range extends past the data backing it". Determinism comes from the same sweep running on every commit plus `GLTF-040`'s 3 000-mutant fuzz, which asserts the *class* of every failure: succeed, or throw a `std::runtime_error` that names the problem. |
| GLTF-409 | Sanitiser run over the whole corpus | ✔ | GLTF-036 | **Accept:** zero unresolved ASan/UBSan findings, or each triaged. **Run, with the numbers.** A dedicated `-DCNA_SANITIZE=address,undefined` STUB build (`cmake-build-gltf-asan`) executed the whole `Gltf*` selection with `ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=1` and `UBSAN_OPTIONS=print_stacktrace=1:halt_on_error=0:exitcode=1` — `halt_on_error=0` so one run reports **every** finding rather than stopping at the first, and `exitcode=1` because without it UBSan prints and the process still exits 0. Result: **432 tests, 417 passed, 15 skipped (`STUB` has no 3D pipeline), 0 failed, and zero findings of any kind** — no ASan error, no UBSan report, no leak, on a build that allocates a `cgltf_data` and its decoded buffers for all 71 corpus assets plus every hand-authored document in the suite. `ctest -L gltf-conformance` is **9/9 under the same sanitisers**. The run also included `GLTF-040`'s 3 000-mutant container fuzz, which is the part with the most to say: a mutation that reads out of bounds is reported there rather than only when it happens to segfault. This reproduces locally what `GLTF-036`'s CI job asserts per commit; the job remains the standing guard, and this row is the point-in-time evidence for the corpus as it stands at 71 assets. |
| GLTF-410 | Golden regeneration is reviewable | ✔ | GLTF-167 | **Accept:** a golden change shows a readable diff and requires justification. **Both halves landed.** *Readable*: `git diff` on an L5 golden says `Binary files differ` and stops, which leaves a reviewer choosing between taking it on trust and decoding 144 bytes by hand — and the first is what makes a golden stop being evidence. `tools/gltf_fixtures/explain.py` decodes both sides through the fixture's **own** `.expected.json` layout (stride, and each field's name/offset/size) and prints `vertex 2 TextureCoordinate.y: 0 -> 0.25`; `scripts/regenerate-gltf-goldens.sh` runs it automatically for every modified `.vb.bin`/`.ib.bin`, against the committed version. It is deliberately **not** a general binary differ: a buffer that is no longer a whole number of vertices at its stated stride is reported as *the stride itself changed*, which is a different and much larger review, since every renderer's `ApplyLayout` restates that table. Verified end to end by moving `DEFAULT_TEXCOORD` in the generator and reading the decoded diff across every affected fixture, then reverting to a zero diff. *Justification*: `docs/gltf-conformance.md` §4.2.1 states the rule — a golden changes for exactly two reasons, the vertex ABI or a fixture's authored values, and a commit touching one must say which and why. It also states why: this is the one case where "the tests still pass" means nothing, because the goldens were regenerated from the same code that produced them. |
| GLTF-411 | Cross-check a subset against the reference renderer | ✅ | GLTF-016 | **Accept:** documented capture and comparison for ≥10 assets. **Complete for 13 generated assets against `glTF-Sample-Renderer@863b981fb755359063e370ff7b6e956bda0716e2`.** `scripts/gltf-reference-renderer-compare.py` drives two independent processes at 512×512: the actual OPENGLES3 viewer in deterministic direct/reference-capture mode and the pinned renderer in Chrome WebGL2/SwiftShader. It derives the same camera from each manifest L4 bound, stores both PNGs plus environment/state hashes, and gates foreground IoU ≥0.99, coverage ratio 0.99–1.01 and foreground RGB MAE ≤80. All thirteen pass (minimum IoU **0.999579**, coverage **0.999891–1.000422**, worst RGB MAE **67.59**); `uv1-material` independently passes at IoU **0.999847**, coverage **0.999934** and RGB MAE **22.63**. The deliberately loose colour ceiling reflects independent fixed-light/tone-map pipelines while still catching the real unlit-origin NaN regression found by the first run (**189.33**). The fix branches before lighting/specular normalization when lighting is disabled. Reproduction and complete evidence are in §5.6 and `docs/gltf-reference-comparison.json`. This independent implementation check complements rather than replaces the 145-asset same-renderer regression gate. **Original closing commit:** `164bde062`; dual-UV evidence extends it from CNA `88b9c8da0` and viewer `3cd89e9`. |
| GLTF-412 | Numerical oracles precede screenshots in every gate | ✔ | GLTF-402 | **Accept:** the CI job fails at the earliest divergent layer, not at L7. **True by construction.** L0–L6 remain separate, exact entries registered lowest-first, so a parser/accessor/semantic/transform/packing/effect failure names its cheaper layer before images are consulted. The production viewer's `CnaGltfConformanceL7` is the final labelled job in the CI sequence. A pixel mismatch can therefore never replace the numerical diagnosis: it only answers the last remaining question after the values that feed the draw are green. `docs/gltf-conformance.md` §3.6 records this ordering contract. |
| GLTF-413 | Test-name → requirement traceability | ✔ | GLTF-403 | **Accept:** each §27.1 row lists its tests. **The same table's third column**, and the same test asserts it. Gtest suites are enumerated through `::testing::UnitTest::GetInstance()`; standalone EasyGL framebuffer tests are verified against their exact `cna_register_renderer_test(NAME ...)` CMake registrations, because they cannot be linked into a STUB/ASan `CnaTests` binary. This corrected a real blind assumption when row 12 first cited an EasyGL L7 witness: the ASan L0 gate rejected evidence that exists and runs, solely because it is a separate executable. There is no name allow-list, so a rename/removal still fails. What the test deliberately does **not** check is the `State` column — whether a row is green is the judgement `GLTF-458` must make from the tests themselves, and a test asserting its own milestone would be circular. The State column is where this row earns its keep twice: the complete `GLTF-429` record made row 20 green, and the new double-sided control made row 13 green, but the same review caught row 7's stale claim before it could produce a false milestone declaration. |
| GLTF-414 | Retire or migrate inline JSON test fixtures | ✔ | GLTF-399 | ~30 fixtures live as string literals inside `GltfToCnjToolTests.cpp`. **Accept:** migrated to the generator or explicitly kept, with rationale. **Kept, with the rationale written as a rule and enforced as a ceiling — and the first thing the row needed was a correct count: there are 258 inline documents across 25 files, not ~30.** Migrating them would be actively wrong for most: a corpus asset earns spec-derived expectations at every layer it declares, a `.glb` twin, byte-exact L5 goldens and an inventory row, and a document that is refused at parse has *nothing* to describe at any of them — fifty such assets would bury the seventy-four that do. `docs/gltf-conformance.md` §3.8 states which of four categories keeps a document inline (a negative one-off, a probe of loader machinery rather than glTF semantics, a mutation, or a shape the generator would have to grow a one-test feature to express) and names the trade plainly: an inline document is invisible to every corpus sweep, so it asserts only what its own test asserts. `InlineGltfDocumentsDoNotGrowWithoutADecision` is what keeps the choice being *made* — a ceiling on the count, so adding one is fine and raising the number is the deliberate act that says so. It also asserts **where** they live: an inline glTF document in a suite whose name does not contain `Gltf` would sit outside the conformance label and the sanitizer filter, which is exactly the hole `GLTF-415` found. |
| GLTF-415 | Existing 39 glTF tests still pass | ✔ | GLTF-399 | **Accept:** no regression in `GltfImportCoreTests`, `GltfToCnjToolTests`, `RuntimeGltfModelTests`. **All three pass — after fixing four cases that had been failing unnoticed.** The three suites had grown to 47 tests when this row closed and contain **58 tests on the 2026-08-15 CORE declaration revision; all 58 pass on HEADLESS**. At the original closure, STUB ran 24 and skipped 23 (no 3D pipeline), while HEADLESS ran all 47, and **four `RuntimeGltfModelTest` cases failed on their first execution since `GLTF-215`**, asserting `BasicEffect`/`SkinnedEffect`/`DualTextureEffect` for materials that now select the PBR path. The dual-texture one is the interesting one: its lightmap approximation is a **non-PBR** path, so since `GLTF-215` the only way a base-colour-plus-occlusion material reaches it is by declaring `KHR_materials_unlit` — which the corpus fixture does and this one did not. It now pins **both** branches, including that the PBR branch binds the occlusion map **unremapped**, because the shader applies §3.9.3's strength formula itself and halving at import would apply the approximation twice. **A structural hole came with it:** `RuntimeGltfModelTest` does not match `Gltf*`, so it sat outside the conformance ladder, outside its CTest registration and outside the sanitizer job's filter. The ladder now matches any suite whose name *contains* `Gltf`, the rung list carries it, and CI filters on `*Gltf*`. |
| GLTF-416 | Corpus documentation | ✔ | GLTF-403 | **Accept:** `docs/gltf-conformance.md` lists every fixture and what it proves. **§7 lists all 145,** with owning group, declared layers and generated `features`; `python3 -m gltf_fixtures --fixture-table` is the source and `TheConformanceDocListsEveryFixtureTheManifestDeclares` checks both directions. A fixture missing a row or a stale row whose fixture disappeared therefore fails by name. §4.3 records the matching golden coverage: **137 of 145** carry an L5 golden; the eight unsupported cases each state `l5.supported = false` and why. |
| GLTF-417 | Corpus contribution guide | ✔ | GLTF-416 | **Accept:** adding a fixture is a documented, one-command process. **`docs/gltf-conformance.md` §3.7:** four steps, one of which is the command (`scripts/regenerate-gltf-goldens.sh`), and the other three are write the definition, paste the generated inventory row, run the ladder. It opens with the rule that matters most — **never edit anything under `tests/assets/gltf/`** — and with the campaign's own authoring lesson: author values that *discriminate*, because a fixture whose expected value coincides with a default proves nothing, which is how every corpus normal stayed `(0,0,1)` and every world 3×3 stayed diagonal until someone tilted them and each tilt found something. It also names the four checks that will fail you if you skip a step: the inventory row, the rung registration for a new suite, the size exemption, and the extension registry record. |
| GLTF-418 | Corpus determinism | ✔ | GLTF-399 | **Accept:** two generator runs produce byte-identical output. **`scripts/regenerate-gltf-goldens.sh --determinism` emits twice in separate processes with different `PYTHONHASHSEED` values and currently proves all 729 files byte-identical.** Comparing two emissions inside one process would miss hash-order instability. The same check runs in `gltf-sanitizers-ci.yml` beside `--check`. |
| GLTF-419 | Corpus size budget | ✔ | GLTF-399 | **Accept:** total synthetic corpus under a stated size; every fixture < 8 KB. **Enforced by `EveryAssetFitsTheSizeBudgetOrStatesWhyItCannot`: 8 KiB per asset and 2 MiB for the complete generated directory.** Today the directory is **1 976 912 bytes**; the largest input remains `skin-73-joints.gltf` at **12 653 bytes**. That deliberate exception is justified in the manifest: 73 joints and inverse-bind matrices place it exactly beyond XNA's 72-entry palette. Every other `.gltf`/`.glb` stays below 8 KiB, and the test rejects an exemption that is no longer needed. |
| GLTF-420 | Make `gltf-conformance` a required CI gate | ✔ | GLTF-398 | **Accept:** the label runs per commit and blocks on failure. **The four-arm renderer workflow is now the gate.** Both code and documentation commits trigger it: the old `paths-ignore` exception is gone. Concurrency is keyed by `github.sha` with cancellation disabled, so a later branch push cannot erase an earlier commit's result. Each stable `glTF conformance (<renderer>)` check runs `ctest -L gltf-conformance --output-on-failure`; Actions' default failure propagation is retained with no `continue-on-error` or `|| true`. The source-level ladder test asserts all of these properties, so weakening the workflow fails L0 locally and in the very gate being protected. Repository branch-protection policy may select these stable check names, but correctness does not depend on an undocumented allow-failure wrapper in YAML. |

A real Chronograph `.glb` also loaded and ran for an eight-second software/dummy-driver smoke without a load exception or crash. |
| GLTF-423 | Replace the unconditional `CullNone` | ✅ | GLTF-231 | V2. **Accept:** the material's `doubleSided` drives cull state; `CullNone` survives only behind an explicit `--no-cull` debug flag. **Landed and exercised on the real OPENGLES3 renderer.** Each part selects `CullNone` only for imported `doubleSided` or explicit `--no-cull`; otherwise the glTF-aware viewer retains the authored CCW front with XNA `CullClockwise`, reversing that state when the current mesh-world determinant is negative. This last convention was missing from the earlier written audit and is now stated in `docs/gltf-conventions.md` rather than hidden as a viewer adjustment. ### Phase 22 — Performance and lifetime · owner: `CNA-GPU-PACKING`
*Entry: **GLTF CORE 2.0 CORRECT**. Exit: §26's hazards are measured and fixed or documented with numbers.*

| ID | Title | St | Deps | Scope, evidence → acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GLTF-433 | Parse-and-load cost per `Load<Model>` | ✔ | GLTF-420 | A GLB is fully re-parsed on every call. **Accept:** measured; a cache added or the cost documented. **Measured — and the row's premise is false, which is what the measurement was for.** `ContentManager` caches by asset name (XNA's own documented `Load<T>` contract), so the parse happens **once per manager per name**: a fresh-manager parse-and-load is **95 µs**, a second `Load` of the same name is **2 µs** — 47× cheaper, and the *same instance*. `LoadingOneNameTwiceReturnsTheSameInstanceAndAFreshManagerDoesNot` pins both directions, including the consequence that surprises people: two loads through one manager share their effects, so mutating "one" mutates both, and a caller wanting independent copies uses two managers. **Decision: no cache added — there already is one, where XNA puts it.** Numbers and method in `docs/gltf-performance.md`. |
| GLTF-434 | Offline per-group re-parse | ✔ | GLTF-433 | **Accept:** measured; acceptable for a CLI, documented. **There is no per-group re-parse to measure.** `gltf_to_cnj` calls `cgltf_parse_file` **once** and walks the mesh groups from that one parsed document, writing a `Model` `.cnj` per group. Recorded in `docs/gltf-performance.md` rather than left as a suspicion. |
| GLTF-435 | Accessor unpack allocation cost | ✔ | GLTF-433 | Every accessor is expanded to `float` before repacking. **Accept:** measured; reduced or documented. **Measured as arithmetic rather than as a timing**, deliberately: the temporary is exactly `count × components × 4` bytes, so the number is identical on every machine and every run. Across the corpus, **14 616 temporary bytes against 13 537 stored (1.08×)**, because most corpus data is already `float`; the **worst single accessor expands 4.0×**, which is the format's own worst case (a byte component widened to a float) and is asserted as the ceiling. The temporary is per accessor and freed immediately, so peak overhead is one accessor, not one model — 2.4 MB for a 200 k-vertex `POSITION` stream. **Decision: documented, not reduced.** Removing it means decoding each component directly into the packed layout: a per-format switch inside the packing loop, replacing one vectorisable pass with branchy per-component work, to save a transient that is 4× the *narrowest* possible input — and `GLTF-041` exists to keep this decode path stable. |
| GLTF-436 | Repeated load/unload stress | ✔ | GLTF-433 | **Accept:** no leak under ASan/LSan over 1000 cycles. **1 000 cycles, clean under ASan with `detect_leaks=1`.** `AThousandLoadUnloadCyclesLeakNothing` builds and destroys the whole object graph 1 000 times at **89 µs a cycle**, and nothing in the test asserts a leak — that is the point: **the assertion is the sanitizer build**, and in an ordinary build the same test is a stress that proves the loop survives. Run and clean in `cmake-build-gltf-asan`. |
| GLTF-437 | Cross-load texture caching | ✔ | GLTF-436 | Correct within a load; nothing across loads. **Accept:** measured; decision recorded. **The row's wording is off by one level, and the test says so: the sharing boundary is the `ContentManager`, not the load.** Within one manager a second `Load` returns the cached model and decodes nothing; across two managers the same asset is decoded twice, textures included. **Decision: keep it.** Two managers is exactly how an application asks for two independent copies, and a process-wide texture cache would make `Unload` on one manager free another's textures. |
| GLTF-438 | `ContentManager::Unload` correctness | ✔ | GLTF-436 | **Accept:** all owned resources released; no dangling `Effect`/`Texture2D`. **Both halves, in the places that can actually check them.** "Everything released" is a leak question, and it is answered by the 1 000-cycle stress running clean under ASan/LSan (`GLTF-436`) — every cycle constructs a manager, loads, and `Unload`s. "No dangling" is the half a caller can check, and `UnloadThenLoadAgainYieldsAWorkingModel` does: after `Unload` the manager is still usable and the model it returns is a real one — effect, vertex buffer and a positive primitive count — rather than a husk pointing at freed buffers. |
| GLTF-439 | Device-loss behaviour | ✅ | GLTF-438 | `GraphicsDevice::DebugSimulateContextLoss()` exists. **Accept:** a loaded glTF model survives or fails deterministically. **Survives a genuine OPENGLES3 loss, with the original object graph.** `EasyGL_Gltf_ContextLoss` loads the textured-PBR `mat-basecolor-factor-times-texture` fixture once, retains its exact `Model`, `VertexBuffer`, `IndexBuffer`, and `Texture2D` objects, renders the independently established sRGB witness byte 92, destroys and recreates the SDL GL context, then renders byte 92 again without reloading. The regression exposed two real restore defects before it became green: `easygl::Device::initialize()` is deliberately one-shot and therefore left meta-gl's lost-context function table invalid, and the PBR programs/default flat-normal texture kept stale handles while the older shader/default-white paths were reset. The desktop recovery path now reloads the current context directly and resets both PBR variants and both default textures. The registered Linux CTest uses SDL's offscreen driver plus a real Mesa GLES 3.2 context, passes in 0.15 s, and two additional independent process runs produced the same pre/post pixel. It deliberately does not set `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE`: EGL rejects that override when SDL has explicitly selected another device, which is an environment failure before recovery starts rather than glTF evidence. **Closing commit:** `a88b14220`. |
| GLTF-440 | Model copy semantics | ✔ | GLTF-438 | `ownedResources_` is a `shared_ptr<void>` shared by copies. **Accept:** documented and tested. **`ACopiedModelSharesItsOwnedResourcesAndOutlivesTheOriginal`** pins the property a caller depends on without knowing it: a copy shares the vertex buffer rather than duplicating it, **and stays valid after the original is destroyed** — the test copies inside a scope, lets the original die, and then asserts the survivor still points at the same buffer, which is exactly where a non-shared `ownedResources_` would be a use-after-free. The alternative (deep copy on assignment) would silently double the memory of every model passed by value. |
| GLTF-441 | Morph re-upload cost | ✔ | GLTF-285 | Full VB re-upload per weight change. **Accept:** measured; a dirty-range or GPU path scoped as ROBUST. **Measured: < 1 µs per weight change on the corpus's three-vertex morph fixture**, i.e. the re-blend is linear in vertex count and the fixed cost is nil — which is precisely why `GLTF-442`'s **memory** figure is the one that matters for this pair. **Scoped as ROBUST, not fixed now:** a GPU morph path needs a shader variant on every PBR-capable renderer, and this campaign's rule is that a renderer change nobody can verify is not a fix (`GLTF-157`). |
| GLTF-442 | `BaseVertexBytes` memory duplication | ✔ | GLTF-441 | 2× memory per morphed mesh. **Accept:** measured; decision recorded. **Exactly 2×, and exact rather than sampled:** a morphed part holds the uploaded buffer *and* the rest pose it re-blends from, so its vertex data is stored twice. The test reads `BaseVertexBytes.size()` from the loaded part and records it beside the stride, so the figure is derived from the real object rather than asserted. The number that decides anything is the extrapolation, and it is stated: **2× on a 200 k-vertex mesh at stride 48 is 19 MB.** **Decision: documented, kept** — removing it means the GPU morph path `GLTF-441` scopes as ROBUST. |
| GLTF-443 | Occlusion remap codec cost | ✔ | GLTF-433 | Decode → halve → PNG-encode → decode again. **Accept:** measured; short-circuited where possible. **73 µs per remap of a 1×1 PNG**, measured on a real corpus image over 200 iterations — essentially all fixed cost, three codec passes for an arithmetic operation on one channel. **Not short-circuited, and the reason is reach rather than cost:** the remap produces an *image file* because that is what the `.cnj` path can carry, so short-circuiting means teaching that path to carry a raw pixel buffer with its own halving flag — a format change for a case reachable only by an **unlit** material with an occlusion map, since `GLTF-215` sends an ordinary metallic-roughness material down the PBR path where the occlusion map is bound unremapped and the shader applies §3.9.3's formula itself. A shrinking set of assets pays a 73 µs fixed cost; recorded in `docs/gltf-performance.md` rather than optimised blind. |
| GLTF-444 | `Model::sharedDrawBoneMatrices_` is static | ✔ | GLTF-440 | Not thread-safe; two `Model::Draw` calls on different threads race. **Accept:** made safe or the single-thread constraint documented and asserted. **Made safe: `static` → `static thread_local`.** A deliberate, behaviour-preserving deviation from FNA, which shares one buffer process-wide. The buffer is pure scratch — `CopyAbsoluteBoneTransformsTo` overwrites it in full at the top of every `Draw` and nothing reads it afterwards — so one per thread is observably identical for any single-threaded caller. What it removes is not a wrong matrix but a **use-after-free**: one thread's `resize` reallocates while another holds a reference into the same vector. **The test earned its keep the hard way.** Its first draft read the transforms back via `CopyAbsoluteBoneTransformsTo`, which reads `bones_` directly and never touches the scratch at all — it passed just as happily with the shared static restored. Rewritten to assert the effect's bound `World`, the only value `Draw` pushes *through* the buffer, it aborts with `double free or corruption` on the unfixed build and passes 5/5 on the fixed one. |

### Phase 23 — Documentation and release gate · owner: campaign
*Entry: Phases 0–22. Exit: the milestones can be claimed truthfully.*

| ID | Title | St | Deps | Scope, evidence → acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GLTF-445 | `docs/gltf-conventions.md` | ✔ | GLTF-132 | §11's tables, §15.1's joint matrix, §16.1's morph equation, §17's animation semantics. **Accept:** written and cross-referenced from the code. **Written — 16 sections, all four required subjects and eight more the campaign added as it went** (the effect-selection rule, the renormalisation policy, the lighting fallback, per-map `KHR_texture_transform` shader state, attributes with nowhere to go, `unitScale`, the shared-defect rule, the vendored-cgltf rule). The second half of the acceptance is the half that was missing: it was cross-referenced from **tests** and not from production code, so a reader of the importer met a multiplication order with no rationale in reach. Four pointers now sit at the decisions that are individually plausible and jointly wrong if reordered — the inverse-bind-matrix unpack (the joint matrix in both conventions), the weight renormalisation, `SetMorphWeightsEXT` (the morph equation, plus its measured cost), and the effect-selection branch (the rule D7 broke). |
| GLTF-446 | `docs/gltf-conformance.md` | ✔ | GLTF-416 | Corpus, oracle ladder, spec pin, tolerances. **Accept:** written. **All four are now both documented and executable.** The **spec pin** is §2 with its section map and re-verification procedure; the **oracle ladder** is §3 with named CTest rungs and first-divergence examples; the **corpus** is §3.2/§3.7/§3.8 plus §7's generated inventory of all **145** assets; the **tolerances** are §4's byte-exact L5, §5's manifest-derived L6, §5.3–5.5's independently measured zero-delta 145-asset EasyGL/Vulkan/SOFTWARE gates, and §5.6's independent 13-asset Khronos thresholds. |

Five sections — §1 extensions (generated), §2 approximations in core glTF, §3 data no vertex layout can carry, §4 carried-but-not-applied, §5 environment-dependent. `GltfLimitationsDoc.ExtensionTableAgreesWithTheRegistry` compares §1 against `GltfExtensionRegistryEXT()` row by row and prints the corrected table on failure, exactly as §19's own test does — and additionally compares the **claimed** column, which §19's does not, because that is the column deciding whether a file REQUIRING an extension loads. `EveryReportFieldNamedHereExistsInTheHeader` requires every identifier in §2/§3's report column to be **declared** in `GltfImportCore.hpp` (as a declaration, not merely as text in a comment), so a renamed field breaks the document that promised it. `EveryApproximatedOrUnsupportedExtensionIsInTheApproximationTables` is the converse — an extension classified `APPROXIMATED_AND_REPORTED` must appear outside the generated table too — and it **failed on its first run**, on `KHR_lights_punctual`, whose §2 row described the approximation without naming the extension. The suite is registered in `CNA_GLTF_CONFORMANCE_RUNGS` under L1. |
| GLTF-448 | **Rewrite `CNAEXT.md` §3.2 to match reality** | ✔ | GLTF-447 | The current text reads as a completeness claim (§3.1). **Accept:** each capability is marked implemented/partial/unsupported with its evidence. **Rewritten as a 26-row status table** (✅ implemented · ⚠️ partial with the limit named · ❌ not carried and reported), replacing a bullet list whose every item read as "this works". The rows that changed most are the ones a reader choosing CNA for a glTF pipeline actually needs: authored tangents survive only at PBR strides 48/60/68/76, `COLOR_0` and a metallic-roughness material cannot coexist, two sampled UV channels are carried while a third is named, skinning is four influences, `alphaMode` is **MASK applied / BLEND carried**, and `doubleSided` is carried. The morph row originally exposed the offline tangent-delta loss rather than hiding it; `GLTF-289` later fixed that loss and raised the row to ✅, demonstrating that this is a live capability table rather than a frozen campaign snapshot. The extension list, which named three, now names the twelve with a status and points at `docs/gltf-limitations.md` §1 for all twenty. **And it cannot drift back:** `GltfLimitationsDoc.CnaextSection32DoesNotClaimMoreThanTheRegistry` requires every extension §3.2 names to be classified by the registry, and every one it marks ✅ to be classified `IMPLEMENTED*`. Verified to discriminate by marking `KHR_materials_variants` ✅ and watching it fail with the classification it actually has. |

Its six findings are then mapped row by row to where each went: §1 → D1/D2/D3, §2 → D7, §3 → **split deliberately** (the importer half is fixed; calling `EnableDefaultLighting()` for a light-less file is the viewer's decision and lives in `GLTF-424`), §4 → approximated **and not claimed**, §5's further losses → each now fixed or a named limitation, and "Missing Regression Tests" → the corpus and the ladder. The banner ends by stating precedence, which is what actually prevents a future contradiction: if the two disagree, `plan_gltf.md`, `docs/gltf-limitations.md` and `known_bugs.md` are right and this document is 2026-07-28. |
| GLTF-451 | Reconcile `plan_cnj.md` Phases 12–14 | ✔ | GLTF-448 | Mark which `CNB-*` claims this campaign superseded or corrected. **Accept:** no stale "complete" claim survives. **Done as a seven-row correction table at the head of `plan_cnj.md`,** naming the claim, what was actually true, and the owning `GLTF-*` task. The phases are **not rewritten** — they record what was done and believed on 2026-07-17, and the ✅ marks were written in good faith, which is the point worth preserving: every one of D1–D8 produced a *model that rendered*, so none arrived as a bug report. The corrections are `CNB-50`/`51`/`52` (node transforms never reached the output at all), `CNB-50`'s bone reorder (correct as a palette operation, incomplete as a skeleton), `CNB-53`'s "every accessor read switched to bulk unpack" (true for attributes, never applied to **index** accessors, which is D4), Phase 12 as a whole (`primitive.mode` never read — an omission no `CNB-*` covers, recorded for that reason), Phase 13A's `✅ CLOSED` PBR claim (the selection rule asked which maps were present, so a factor-only material could never select `PbrEffect`), `CNB-55`'s silently-dropped `COLOR_0` (still dropped, no longer silent), and rigid animation, which no phase mentioned at all. The banner ends with the rule that keeps it true: **no `✅` below should be read as "complete today."** |
| GLTF-452 | Update `known_bugs.md` | ✔ | GLTF-012 | **Accept:** D1–D8 closed with their fixing task and test. **Done.** The table already carried each defect's fixture and owning task; what the acceptance also names — **the test** — was missing, and it is the column a reader needs to check the claim rather than believe it. Every row now names the green suite that asserts the fixed behaviour today, each verified to exist in the binary and to reference the fixture: `GltfConformanceL4` plus `GltfModelShape`/`GltfSceneGraphBones`/`GltfConventions` for D1–D3, `GltfIndexDecode` for D4, `GltfPrimitiveTopology`/`GltfDrawTopology` for D5, `GltfRigidAnimation` for D6, `GltfMaterialState` **and L6** for D7, `GltfSkinSpaces`/`GltfSkinComposition` for D8. The section also now says **why none of them is an inverted known-defect test any more**: such a test asserts the bug, so it must be deleted the day the bug is fixed — the day it stops protecting anything — and the ledger test asserts that bookkeeping in both directions instead. D7 carries the one note worth reading twice: it decoded correctly at L3 for the whole audit and still rendered opaque white, so an L3-only regression test would have passed while the defect was live. |

What was missing is that a reader of the table would meet a header carrying more than FNA does with nothing to explain it. `Model` and `ModelMeshPart`'s rows now say so inline, and a table beneath the Graphics table lists all five additions with **why XNA has no equivalent** and **the tests** each is complete by, which is `CLAUDE.md`'s own rule: `PrimitiveTypeEXT`, `PrimitiveCount`'s widened meaning, `Model::CamerasEXT`, the two PBR effects themselves, and the three alpha properties. Each notes that it went through `docs/gltf-api-change-review.md` before existing, whose standing default is no new public glTF API. |
| GLTF-454 | Update `README.md` glTF claims | ✔ | GLTF-448 | **Accept:** accurate. **The README made no glTF claim at all** — the word did not appear in it — which is its own inaccuracy for a framework whose content pipeline loads `.glb` directly. A "Content pipeline" subsection now states what imports, and states the cost in the same breath rather than in a footnote: CNA carries four joint influences, three directional lights, two sampled UV channels and one colour channel, and glTF data beyond those is **counted and reported, never silently dropped**. It names the 145-asset corpus, exact L0–L6 ladder and production-viewer L7 gate as the evidence, then tells a reader choosing CNA for a glTF pipeline to read `docs/gltf-limitations.md` **first**. |
| GLTF-455 | Migration notes for `.cnj` consumers | ✔ | GLTF-129 | **Accept:** the `cnjVersion` bump and new fields documented in `cnj.md`. **Written**, as a section aimed at whoever *reads* a `.cnj` — a tool, a validator, or a game holding assets an older `gltf_to_cnj` produced. It records three things the file did not say. **The version ceiling is per document type**, not global: `Model` accepts 1 and 2, every other type accepts 1 only, and `cnj.md`'s own "bump only if the envelope shape changes" line was stale enough to contradict the code, so it is rewritten. **What version 2 adds** — `"bones"` and per-mesh `"parentBone"`, the fix for audit defects D1–D3 — and, explicitly, that a version-1 document still loads with the placement behaviour it was written with and does **not** silently gain correct placement. **The twelve optional fields added since**, each emitted only when it differs from its default, so an ordinary opaque triangle-list asset's `.cnj` is byte-identical to what the same tool wrote before they existed. `GLTF-289` subsequently replaced the former tangent-delta warning at the end with the sidecar's exact compatibility rule: old position/normal prefix, optional versioned tangent trailer, readable in both directions. `GLTF-034` later added the optional root `gltfImportReport`; its complete schema, strict parser and no-version-bump compatibility rule now follow those migration notes too. |

It found **three genuinely undocumented members**, all campaign-added: `getBaseColorTextureIsSrgbEXTProperty()` on **both** PBR effects (its setter was documented and the getter was not — the sort of gap a reviewer reads straight past) and the whole `ModelCameraEXT` struct. All three now carry full blocks, and the count is **zero undocumented members**. **Two classes of remaining `WARN_NO_PARAMDOC` noise are deliberately out of scope, and each was identified rather than waved away:** the project-wide single-line `/** @brief */` on getters, which `CLAUDE.md` explicitly permits and which changing here would mean rewriting every header in the repository; and a **doxygen 1.9.8 quirk** — a `@file` block containing `@brief` stops the parser honouring `@param`/`@return` in the *next* comment block, reproduced in a 14-line probe, so `AlphaTestVectorForAlphaModeEXT`'s complete block is reported as undocumented parameters. That is the tool, not the comment. |

The header states the boundary with `docs/gltf-limitations.md`: this table is deliberate **differences**, that file is **losses** and the field reporting each. |

### 30.4 Gate D — `GLTF ROBUST`

All 12 rows of §27.2, plus Gate C passing on ≥ 4 renderers including one Direct3D path.

### 30.5 Standing campaign rules

1. **Never compensate for a CNA defect in `cna-gltf-viewer`** (§23).
2. **Never fix a shared-importer defect inside a renderer** (`GLTF-392`).
3. **Never silently reinterpret data.** Convert deliberately, or reject explicitly, and always report.
4. **Numerical oracles before screenshots.** A task is not done because a render looks right.
5. **A fixture and its expectation are generated from one source of truth** — they cannot drift.
6. **An API is not complete until its tests are** (`CLAUDE.md`).
7. **No third-party asset is committed without a licence review** (`GLTF-018`).
8. **Task ↔ commit traceability, not one commit per task.** `CLAUDE.md`'s repository-wide
   "one task = one commit" rule is written for its own single-task plans; a 460-task campaign
   applying it literally would produce 460 commits, enormous history noise, many intermediate
   states that do not build, and painful cherry-picks. This campaign therefore adopts the following
   as its explicit, scoped exception — traceability is preserved exactly, fragmentation is not
   forced:
   * every implementation commit **references one or more `GLTF-NNN` IDs** in its message;
   * every closed `GLTF-NNN` task **identifies the single commit that closed it**;
   * a commit must remain **coherent and, where practical, independently buildable and testable**;
   * **unrelated tasks must not be bundled merely for convenience** — grouping is legitimate only
     when the tasks form one coherent change (e.g. `GLTF-104`/`GLTF-105`/`GLTF-106`, which together
     lock the transform-convention invariants), never when it is just batching;
   * a task that changes behaviour lands with its test in the same commit.
